11. Concurrency (sequentialised with a recorded schedule; the single-
    thread assumption of §3.2 is the v1 contract), self-modifying code,
    signals, setjmp. Floating point is decided in §3.2 (deterministic
    soft-float in both builds) and is no longer open.
12. Repository hosting and release mechanics for §6.1.

## 10. Process

Vision first (this document), iterate with the council, then design, then
spikes, then code. The current Bennett.jl and BennettVM.jl are frozen as
the v1 reference and differential oracle. No reboot code is written on the
strength of this document. Worklog, beads, red-green, exhaustive
verification, no remote CI carry over unchanged.


# The document under review (round 1 snapshot)

# bp-fj1: the §5 reference analysis on the rung-4 SQLite closure, measured (for b-dialect-spec-5u3)

Status: evidence and amendment proposal for the council, written by the plugin rung-4 stream
(branch `rung4-plugin`, 2026-11-02). Nothing here changes the default build: it follows dialect
§5.1's K alone (advisor ruling on b-dialect-spec-5u3). Results of the earlier closure rules are
labelled NON-REFERENCE wherever they appear.

## 1. Setup

- Program: the rung-4 N16 closure of unmodified SQLite as the plugin's analysis sees it,
  `.tmp/rung4/mod/post-18.ll` in this checkout (sha256 prefix c5b5961480542e34; 1,487 defined
  functions, 285,525 instructions, 36,807 seed slots), LLVM 18, `lto<O3>` with the vectoriser on.
- Command (`.tmp/rung4/sq2.sh 18 <seconds> <tag>`): `opt -passes=bennett -bennett-dialect=0.2.10
  -bennett-analysis-only -bennett-analysis-trace -bennett-analysis-budget-ms=<s*1000>
  -bennett-analysis-max-rounds=100000000`, `ulimit -v 14000000`, under `perf record -F 29`.
  Flagged runs add `-bennett-analysis-early-chain-close`.
- Shared host: 12 cores, 30 GB, load 10 to 30 during these runs. Wall times are therefore upper
  bounds; user time is given where it matters.

## 2. Solver work this shift (the default, K = reference)

Each change keeps the least fixpoint; `-bennett-analysis-check-delta` and `tests/load_delta.py`
check the incremental PT6 results against full passes.

| sha | change | effect on the closure (default K) |
|---|---|---|
| c6b1902 (K0) | before this shift's speedups | 1500 s: round 1 at position 131,735 of 285,525 |
| 44f50aa (D1) | PT6 per changed slot instead of per object version | 1500 s: round 1 at 94,340 (load ~28; ROOT re-interning now 28% of the profile) |
| 885e84a (D2) | partial-overlap ROOTs as one cached set | round 1 complete at 789 s; budget hit in round 2 |
| 0317dcf (D3) | same for PT8; unions adopt their first set | round 1 at 410 s; round 2 complete at 1,407 s; then out of memory |
| afaa89c | constant images per atom; PT8 source lists only when used | not yet measured on the closure |

D2 and D3 reach the identical state after round 1 (1,408,583 slots, 391,155,526 slot atoms), so
the speedups do not change the iteration.

## 3. What the reference fixpoint looks like on SQLite

| run | after round | slots | slot atoms | value atoms | largest value |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D3 (reference) | 0 | 56,312 | 360,391 | 1,754,887 | 2,429 |
| D3 (reference) | 1 | 1,408,583 | 391,155,526 | 56,176,749 | 493,295 |
| D3 (reference) | 2 | 2,643,733 | 1,210,304,346 | 138,617,196 | 247,338 |

D3 then aborted in round 3 with signal 6 at RSS 13.7 GB (the 14 GB address-space limit), 1,965 s
wall, 1,653 s user. Single objects held 92,538 slots and 81,178,844 atoms (`%3` and `%4` in
`@syncJournal`, `@syncJournal.zerobyte`, offsets [-40, 10256]).

The growth is a blob that §5 itself forces, not a plugin imprecision:

- A largest value after round 1 holds 288 objects with 1,820 offsets each. One of them is
  `%2 = alloca i64` in `@pagerOpenWalIfPresent`. Its only escape is `%38 = call i32 %37(ptr %32,
  ptr %2)`, an `sqlite3_io_methods` call. Once AV(%37) is ⊤ (the method pointer is loaded from
  memory the merged pointers already reach), §14.2 makes every address-taken function of type
  `i32 (ptr, ptr)` a candidate, and PT9 binds `%2` to the second parameter of each.
- PT2 is flow-insensitive and applies to every atom of the address. Every field offset used on any
  pointer in the merged set therefore reaches every object in it. That is 1,820 offsets per object
  by round 1, more than 10,000 by round 2, and each offset becomes a slot when stored through.
- §5.1 bounds INT atoms by K = 4096 only. The plugin's reading of the ruling closes the offsets of
  one provenance only past 4096. Nothing bounds the number of provenances in a value or the slots
  of an object. A value can therefore legitimately hold (number of merged objects) × 4096
  explicit atoms, and each object up to tens of thousands of slots.

## 4. The earlier closure rules (NON-REFERENCE, `-bennett-analysis-early-chain-close`)

The flag closes an offset chain past 4 offsets outside the object's literal extent, or past 32
offsets of an object with no literal extent. It also collapses an object past max(64, extent/4)
store-created slots (the rules in b-dialect-spec-5u3).

| run (NON-REFERENCE) | result |
|---|---|
| F1, 7e5c640 (before this shift's speedups) | round 2 at 5,880 s wall, no fixpoint |
| F2, 0317dcf | **fixpoint**: "S1-S5 complete" in 64 rounds, 1,231 s of analysis, 1,279 s wall, 1,255 s user, RSS 2.5 GB; 186,351 slots, 28,306,064 slot atoms, 110,805,551 value atoms, largest value 4,294 |

F2's rounds 11 to 64 each re-ran the same four steps and added two value atoms per round. That is
a slow additive cycle the solver could accelerate; it does not change the fixpoint.

## 5. Assessment

- With the explicit-atom representation, the reference fixpoint does not fit in 14 GB on this
  closure: round 2 alone holds 1.2 × 10^9 slot atoms, and growth continues. The same solver
  reaches the flagged fixpoint in about 20 minutes and 2.5 GB.
- This is not yet a proof that K is intractable for every engine. The blob is close to a product,
  objects × offsets with the same offsets for every object. A factored representation (an object
  set times an offset set, with slots for object-set × offset-range regions) could hold it
  exactly and compactly. Such an engine is a redesign of PT2/PT6/PT7/PT8, not a tuning step. It is
  unproven that its stores and loads stay below the product size, because PT6 still distinguishes
  each overlapped slot.
- Consequences beyond cost: §7.2 candidate sets and §8.4 dispatch domains are computed from these
  sets. A dispatch domain over a value of 10^5 to 10^6 atoms is itself a production-size problem
  for rung 4, whichever engine computes it.

## 6. Proposal for the council (exact changes requested)

Option A, keep K and require nothing new: the plugin builds the factored engine. Cost: a
redesign of the analysis core of unknown duration, with rung 4 blocked meanwhile.

Option B, normative bounds beside K in §5.1, so that every conforming analysis reaches the same
verdicts at a tractable size. Text proposal:

> Offsets of one provenance (X, numerical) in an abstract value are closed when they exceed any
> of: K offsets; 4 offsets outside X's literal extent; 32 offsets when X has no literal extent.
> Closing replaces them with the residue class of the lowest set bit of all their pairwise
> differences, adds (EXT, `*`) and sets the non-handle top flag. An object with more than
> max(64, extent/4) slots created by stores (no literal extent: 64) is collapsed: every later
> access uses (X, `*`), and PT6 of an overlapping load gives ⊤ with explicit ROOT provenance kept.

These are the rules F2 measured. They can only move a verdict toward rejection or a larger
dispatch domain (b-dialect-spec-5u3). Before ratifying them, the council may want the same
measurement for looser constants (for example 16 outside the extent and 256 without one), and
the effect on rung 1 to 3 verdicts. The plugin can run both on request.

Option C, a precision change at the source of the blob instead of a bound: for example
type-filtered parameter binding for ⊤ candidates, or per-call-site candidate pruning by
argument provenance. The measurements above do not cover it.

The plugin's recommendation: B now, with its constants as the council's choice, because it is
measured and keeps all implementations in agreement. A stays open as a longer-term precision
improvement that a later spec version could allow as the reference.

## 7. Fat object (bennett-4ht)

No option changes the host/device boundary. The analysis models the host side through PT11
seeds and EXT. In a mixed fat object only the marked modules form the device closure, and their
exported functions are PT11 boundaries, so the same bounds apply to a smaller closure. The
bounds are per object and per value: none of them assumes the device object is the whole binary.

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

# 🚨 SESSION CLOSE PROTOCOL 🚨

**CRITICAL**: Before saying "done" or "complete", you MUST run this checklist:

```
[ ] 1. bd close <id1> <id2> ...   (close completed issues)
[ ] 2. run quality gates        (tests, linters, builds when relevant)
[ ] 3. git status               (check what changed)
[ ] 4. follow active profile    (conservative: report handoff; team-maintainer: commit/sync/push if enabled)
```

**Policy:** Conservative is the default. Commit, sync, or push only when the active user, orchestrator, or repository profile grants that authority.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

## Common Workflows

**Starting work:**
```bash
bd ready           # Find available work
bd show <id>       # Review issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim it
```

**Completing work:**
```bash
bd close <id1> <id2> ...    # Close all completed issues at once
git status                  # Check changed files
# Conservative/minimal/default: report status and proposed commands; wait for approval
# Team-maintainer opt-in only, unless current instructions forbid it:
# git add . && git commit -m "..."
# git push
```

**Creating dependent work:**
```bash
# Run bd create commands in parallel (use subagents for many items)
bd create --title="Implement feature X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=feature
bd create --title="Write tests for X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task
bd dep add beads-yyy beads-xxx  # Tests depend on Feature (Feature blocks tests)
```

> **Note**: AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md are independent files (not symlinked and not sharing an inode). Mirror substantive edits across both, or symlink one to the other.

# spec/

`Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md` is draft **0.2.12** (2026-11-01), from eight consumer-named gaps,
awaiting council review. It supersedes accepted draft 0.2.11 (round 10, sol/codex);
accepted 0.2.10 is retained in git, with binding interpretations in
[`errata-0.2.10.md`](errata-0.2.10.md). Binding interpretations of 0.2.12 itself (text and SHA
unchanged) are in [`errata-0.2.12.md`](errata-0.2.12.md). The reviewer disposition table is in
[`../docs/briefs/2026-11-01-dialect-0212-consumer-gaps.md`](../docs/briefs/2026-11-01-dialect-0212-consumer-gaps.md).
It targets
ISA 0.8 (`../bennett-isa-spec/spec/`, settled) and is graded against it. `bennett_markers.h` is the
normative C/C++ header of the marker ABI (section 3.3). `Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.2.PROGRESS.md` is
the writing job's log. Council records and the runner live in the `bennett` meta repo
(`docs/council/`, `tools/council.py`); the rubric is `docs/council/rubric-isa.md`, under which any
narrowing of north-star scope is S0.

Rules for changing it:

- **No narrowing** (CLAUDE.md rule 10). A compile-time rejection needs a row in section 22.1 citing
  the north-star clause or maintainer decision that requires it. A deferral (section 24) postpones
  mechanics only and states the capability it keeps. A capability ISA 0.8 cannot express is a
  blocker (section 25.1) with the exact ISA change and a `blocked:` verdict, never a rejection.
- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (header and section 23); drafts are not
  compatible with each other. The reference objects and hashes of section 16 depend on the ISA
  version string, the lifting map and the layout, so they change with any of them.
- **Checked examples** (section 1.5, process rules P-1 to P-3):
  - P-1: every rule id has an `accept` example, every rejection a `reject` example, every blocker
    a `blocked` example; Appendix B is the index, and a rule without one fails the check.
  - P-2: an example the checker cannot yet execute fails (`checker-unimplemented: <capability>`);
    no example is removed or weakened to make the checker pass; the freeze gate is all green.
  - P-3: executed results come from independent executors (LLVM tools, native binaries, the Rust
    ISA reference interpreter `bennett-isa-ref`); Python computes expected artefacts only.
- Run `python3 scripts/dialect_check_v02.py` before anyone grades this draft; it must exit 0. (For 0.2 the
  checker is being extended against the Rust interpreter in a separate job; until then the 0.2
  examples were executed by the writing job as its progress file records.)
- Add every new example or stated result as a tagged block, so the checker executes it; an
  untagged `llvm` or `isa` block is an error. A `semantics=` example must be byte-equal to its
  `corpus/legality/cases/spec-*.ll` file; run `python3 corpus/legality/harness/run.py baseline
  --majors 18,20,23 --cases 'cases/spec-*'` (headless, CLAUDE.md rule 11) after changing one.
- Write derivations out before stating numbers; every stated example result must be computed.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every time it was tried (ISA
  rounds 1-8).
- Revisions go back through the council.
# Binding interpretations of dialect 0.2.12

Published 2026-11-02. Text of draft 0.2.12 at `c775e6d458006fde143f2bf8ec62f07b034fb431`, spec SHA-256 `cfcce70e84ead8a05924c0921f13fc9cb477fdd71f5448c7f6f357ea91349e83`, is unchanged; the full version string, map 0.2.9 and consumer pins remain valid. These interpretations clarify that text without changing any reference result. Draft 0.3 carries the same text in §§5.1, 5.2 and 24.

## 5u3 — §§5.1, 5.2, 24 (council run `contract-5u3-2026-11-02`, round 1 sol and codex accept; `accept-4ht-recheck-2026-11-02` round 1 PASS; muse accept in `accept-muse-batch-2026-11-02`; bead `b-dialect-spec-5u3`)

**§5.1, after K.** The reference bounds INT atoms as above; it imposes no additional
cardinality bound on pointer-offset sets or memory intervals. Implementers
MAY use exact symbolic sets, shared interval relations and SCC
acceleration instead of enumerating represented offsets or slots.
All reference sets and byte/representation-width distinctions must be
preserved for verdicts, candidate sets and dispatch domains. A coarser
summary is not the reference result merely because it is sound for
aliasing. An unfinished analysis reports an unfinished build with the
rule and exhausted implementation resource, not a source rejection,
completed verdict or run-time fault for a valid input.

**§5.2, after the least-fixpoint paragraph.** In a pure modular-translation inclusion SCC, if every cycle transfer is
an unfiltered fixed displacement at one bit width W and preserves one
base/provenance coordinate, close the generated displacements as a
subgroup of Z/(2^W). For a single self-cycle of stride s, each seed a
denotes the coset a modulo gcd(s,2^W). Union distinct seed cosets. For
multiple vertices, retain vertex-specific path displacements; do not
merge their seed coordinates. For edges u→v labelled d_uv,
choose root-to-vertex path displacements p_v and let
g=gcd(2^W,{p_u+d_uv−p_v}). A seed a at u contributes
{a−p_u+p_v+kg mod 2^W} at v. Union contributions from all seeds.
Apply the same rule to a memory relation
only when every transfer preserves the complete storage interval and
representation width and all PT6–PT8 read/overlap results are identical
to the represented individual slots. Retain every ROOT and FN alternative
explicitly. Otherwise use the original rules or another exact method.
PT2's pointer-index width and preserved high pointer bits still apply;
this rule does not replace them by unrestricted full-pointer addition.

**§24.** Analysis representation/acceleration is implementation policy; the
default result is the exact §5 least fixpoint, including existing K=4096
INT abstraction. Time or memory caps do not alter that result. A report
of unfinished analysis keeps the remaining implementation obligation open.
# Mixed fat object — shared contract scope

Proposal for every revision in this directory; not a published ISA amendment.
Clause references are to [ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md),
[the North Star](../../../../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md), and, where
qualified, the maintained dialect 0.2/0.3 texts. This is the shared statement
required by bennett-4ht and the council rubric's standing mixed-fat-object check.

## Citable scoping statement

> A mixed fat object retains an ordinary native host binary and embeds ISA
> objects for marked device modules and their linked device closures (North
> Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). ISA §§9.1–9.3 identify each device object and selected
> root; §12.3 certificates, owner records and reports cover that closure,
> with a separate binding/context set per device root and [] relative to
> that root (§12.1). Native entry, argv/envp and host-only libc need no ISA
> region, frame or certificate record; native comparison binds the selected
> materialised closure, not the whole host. Today §10 launches a device CC
> entry with copied arguments/entropy; §5.4 budgets bind device invocations,
> §3.3/§10 handles belong to a loaded program/run, and §7.3/§10 tapes cover
> device R effects. These require explicit container-to-object/root/certificate
> association, host-entry-to-device-entry bindings, budget/handle lifetime
> negotiation and tape/run association in a versioned mixed-launch contract.
> ISA §7.1 defines device memory but §10 defines no general host-memory-by-reference
> launch ABI: that contract must add address/layout mapping, range/liveness,
> aliasing, ownership/synchronisation, writeback and reversal rules, preserving
> dialect §§6.6, 8.5, 19.4–19.5. Until published, these are explicit
> bennett-4ht boundary obligations, not grounds to require whole-binary
> lowering or omit mixed execution.

## Present assumptions and exact publication changes

| Contract object | Assumption today | Exact change needed for mixed use |
|---|---|---|
| Object and linking (§§9.1–9.3) | One ISA object's closed sections and linked symbols; no native-container schema. | Publish a versioned inventory associating embedded/linked ISA object bytes and object_hash with selected device roots and certificates. A native-container hash, if used, is a separate identity. |
| Certificates/inventory (§§12.1–12.3) | Closure-keyed device-region records; current text does not explicitly delimit native inventory. | Insert the shared scoping rule in §12.3, cross-reference from §9.2, and require complete device-owner/link-proof coverage per root. Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.

# bp-fj1: the §5 reference analysis on the rung-4 SQLite closure, measured (for b-dialect-spec-5u3)

Status: evidence and amendment proposal for the council, written by the plugin rung-4 stream
(branch `rung4-plugin`, 2026-11-02). Nothing here changes the default build: it follows dialect
§5.1's K alone (advisor ruling on b-dialect-spec-5u3). Results of the earlier closure rules are
labelled NON-REFERENCE wherever they appear.

## 1. Setup

- Program: the rung-4 N16 closure of unmodified SQLite as the plugin's analysis sees it,
  `.tmp/rung4/mod/post-18.ll` in this checkout (sha256 prefix c5b5961480542e34; 1,487 defined
  functions, 285,525 instructions, 36,807 seed slots), LLVM 18, `lto<O3>` with the vectoriser on.
- Command (`.tmp/rung4/sq2.sh 18 <seconds> <tag>`): `opt -passes=bennett -bennett-dialect=0.2.10
  -bennett-analysis-only -bennett-analysis-trace -bennett-analysis-budget-ms=<s*1000>
  -bennett-analysis-max-rounds=100000000`, `ulimit -v 14000000`, under `perf record -F 29`.
  Flagged runs add `-bennett-analysis-early-chain-close`.
- Shared host: 12 cores, 30 GB, load 10 to 30 during these runs. Wall times are therefore upper
  bounds; user time is given where it matters.

## 2. Solver work this shift (the default, K = reference)

Each change keeps the least fixpoint; `-bennett-analysis-check-delta` and `tests/load_delta.py`
check the incremental PT6 results against full passes.

| sha | change | effect on the closure (default K) |
|---|---|---|
| c6b1902 (K0) | before this shift's speedups | 1500 s: round 1 at position 131,735 of 285,525 |
| 44f50aa (D1) | PT6 per changed slot instead of per object version | 1500 s: round 1 at 94,340 (load ~28; ROOT re-interning now 28% of the profile) |
| 885e84a (D2) | partial-overlap ROOTs as one cached set | round 1 complete at 789 s; budget hit in round 2 |
| 0317dcf (D3) | same for PT8; unions adopt their first set | round 1 at 410 s; round 2 complete at 1,407 s; then out of memory |
| afaa89c | constant images per atom; PT8 source lists only when used | not yet measured on the closure |

D2 and D3 reach the identical state after round 1 (1,408,583 slots, 391,155,526 slot atoms), so
the speedups do not change the iteration.

## 3. What the reference fixpoint looks like on SQLite

| run | after round | slots | slot atoms | value atoms | largest value |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D3 (reference) | 0 | 56,312 | 360,391 | 1,754,887 | 2,429 |
| D3 (reference) | 1 | 1,408,583 | 391,155,526 | 56,176,749 | 493,295 |
| D3 (reference) | 2 | 2,643,733 | 1,210,304,346 | 138,617,196 | 247,338 |

D3 then aborted in round 3 with signal 6 at RSS 13.7 GB (the 14 GB address-space limit), 1,965 s
wall, 1,653 s user. Single objects held 92,538 slots and 81,178,844 atoms (`%3` and `%4` in
`@syncJournal`, `@syncJournal.zerobyte`, offsets [-40, 10256]).

The growth is a blob that §5 itself forces, not a plugin imprecision:

- A largest value after round 1 holds 288 objects with 1,820 offsets each. One of them is
  `%2 = alloca i64` in `@pagerOpenWalIfPresent`. Its only escape is `%38 = call i32 %37(ptr %32,
  ptr %2)`, an `sqlite3_io_methods` call. Once AV(%37) is ⊤ (the method pointer is loaded from
  memory the merged pointers already reach), §14.2 makes every address-taken function of type
  `i32 (ptr, ptr)` a candidate, and PT9 binds `%2` to the second parameter of each.
- PT2 is flow-insensitive and applies to every atom of the address. Every field offset used on any
  pointer in the merged set therefore reaches every object in it. That is 1,820 offsets per object
  by round 1, more than 10,000 by round 2, and each offset becomes a slot when stored through.
- §5.1 bounds INT atoms by K = 4096 only. The plugin's reading of the ruling closes the offsets of
  one provenance only past 4096. Nothing bounds the number of provenances in a value or the slots
  of an object. A value can therefore legitimately hold (number of merged objects) × 4096
  explicit atoms, and each object up to tens of thousands of slots.

## 4. The earlier closure rules (NON-REFERENCE, `-bennett-analysis-early-chain-close`)

The flag closes an offset chain past 4 offsets outside the object's literal extent, or past 32
offsets of an object with no literal extent. It also collapses an object past max(64, extent/4)
store-created slots (the rules in b-dialect-spec-5u3).

| run (NON-REFERENCE) | result |
|---|---|
| F1, 7e5c640 (before this shift's speedups) | round 2 at 5,880 s wall, no fixpoint |
| F2, 0317dcf | **fixpoint**: "S1-S5 complete" in 64 rounds, 1,231 s of analysis, 1,279 s wall, 1,255 s user, RSS 2.5 GB; 186,351 slots, 28,306,064 slot atoms, 110,805,551 value atoms, largest value 4,294 |

F2's rounds 11 to 64 each re-ran the same four steps and added two value atoms per round. That is
a slow additive cycle the solver could accelerate; it does not change the fixpoint.

## 5. Assessment

- With the explicit-atom representation, the reference fixpoint does not fit in 14 GB on this
  closure: round 2 alone holds 1.2 × 10^9 slot atoms, and growth continues. The same solver
  reaches the flagged fixpoint in about 20 minutes and 2.5 GB.
- This is not yet a proof that K is intractable for every engine. The blob is close to a product,
  objects × offsets with the same offsets for every object. A factored representation (an object
  set times an offset set, with slots for object-set × offset-range regions) could hold it
  exactly and compactly. Such an engine is a redesign of PT2/PT6/PT7/PT8, not a tuning step. It is
  unproven that its stores and loads stay below the product size, because PT6 still distinguishes
  each overlapped slot.
- Consequences beyond cost: §7.2 candidate sets and §8.4 dispatch domains are computed from these
  sets. A dispatch domain over a value of 10^5 to 10^6 atoms is itself a production-size problem
  for rung 4, whichever engine computes it.

## 6. Proposal for the council (exact changes requested)

Option A, keep K and require nothing new: the plugin builds the factored engine. Cost: a
redesign of the analysis core of unknown duration, with rung 4 blocked meanwhile.

Option B, normative bounds beside K in §5.1, so that every conforming analysis reaches the same
verdicts at a tractable size. Text proposal:

> Offsets of one provenance (X, numerical) in an abstract value are closed when they exceed any
> of: K offsets; 4 offsets outside X's literal extent; 32 offsets when X has no literal extent.
> Closing replaces them with the residue class of the lowest set bit of all their pairwise
> differences, adds (EXT, `*`) and sets the non-handle top flag. An object with more than
> max(64, extent/4) slots created by stores (no literal extent: 64) is collapsed: every later
> access uses (X, `*`), and PT6 of an overlapping load gives ⊤ with explicit ROOT provenance kept.

These are the rules F2 measured. They can only move a verdict toward rejection or a larger
dispatch domain (b-dialect-spec-5u3). Before ratifying them, the council may want the same
measurement for looser constants (for example 16 outside the extent and 256 without one), and
the effect on rung 1 to 3 verdicts. The plugin can run both on request.

Option C, a precision change at the source of the blob instead of a bound: for example
type-filtered parameter binding for ⊤ candidates, or per-call-site candidate pruning by
argument provenance. The measurements above do not cover it.

The plugin's recommendation: B now, with its constants as the council's choice, because it is
measured and keeps all implementations in agreement. A stays open as a longer-term precision
improvement that a later spec version could allow as the reference.

## 7. Fat object (bennett-4ht)

No option changes the host/device boundary. The analysis models the host side through PT11
seeds and EXT. In a mixed fat object only the marked modules form the device closure, and their
exported functions are PT11 boundaries, so the same bounds apply to a smaller closure. The
bounds are per object and per value: none of them assumes the device object is the whole binary.

## 8. Artefacts

`.tmp/rung4/prof/trace-{K0,D1,D2,D3,F2}.log`, `perf-*.data`, call-graph samples
`cg-{D2,D3,F2}-N.data` with `cg-*.index`; `.tmp/rung4/logs/sq-*.out`. Not committed (size).

1009:### 5.2 Rules
1019:normalisation before verdicts and dispatch domains freeze; pre-normalisation syntax does
1024:| PT1 | `alloca`, global `@G`, allocation-site call | AV ∋ (X, 0); zero-filled initial extents are seeded as above |
1025:| PT2 | `getelementptr T, ptr p, idx…` | uses P(a), I(a) and the complete finite-width computation of §16.4, also for constant expressions: sequential indices are sign-extended/truncated to I(a), scaling and offset accumulation wrap modulo 2^I(a), and addition preserves pointer bits above I(a). Zero accumulated offset copies AV(p). For every (X,o), include the image of all represented base addresses and index values (unknown base bits range over patterns consistent with DataLayout and X's alignment), using integer/residue-class/`*` offset unions. Unbounded mathematical addition is not a substitute. If an address may leave X, include (EXT, `*`) and the non-handle top flag; PT6 propagates this unknown-address alternative. The unknown-address alternative does not by itself establish host escape (§6.4): retain the original base provenance for that test; actual host/unknown origins keep their restrictions. ROOT provenance and §6.2 restrictions remain explicit. Array dimensions determine stride, never an index bound |
1026:| PT3 | `phi`, `select` (value operands), `freeze`, `addrspacecast`; `bitcast` | the first four copy (union). A bitcast copies lane by lane only when source and destination have identical lane counts and per-lane representation widths, treating a scalar as one lane. Otherwise use the virtual N21 bitcast expansion of §12.6: pack source-lane bit patterns into its prescribed bit string in DataLayout order, then resegment at the destination representation width. Compute output AV through PT4/PT5 transfers of that expansion; finite INT lane sets denote their full Cartesian product, and K applies per resulting lane. Never copy a source-lane INT atom as the value of a packed destination scalar. Unknown bits propagate non-handle top through those transfers. Explicit object and ROOT provenance follows the same expansion and remains subject to §6.2; top never substitutes for ROOT. Apply also to constant expressions and fixed-vscale forms during S2 before verdicts, candidate sets, shapes and dispatch domains freeze. Direct finite bit-string evaluation is equivalent; no LLVM optimisation is required |
1027:| PT4 | `ptrtoint`, `inttoptr`, `zext`/`sext`/`trunc` | copy only bit-preserving conversions. Otherwise apply the actual source/destination-width bit-vector conversion (§16.4): ptrtoint/inttoptr zero-extend or truncate at P(a), sext sign-extends. Use PT2’s represented-address image construction, retaining originating object and ROOT provenance. A narrowed representation that cannot be reassociated with its original object adds (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤; numerical uncertainty is not host escape. Apply also to constant expressions |
1028:| PT5 | `add`/`sub` of a value with pointer atoms and an `INT` set | computes address bits modulo 2^W at the integer operation’s width W, never unbounded offset addition. Relative displacements from the originating base are modular too: zero net displacement represents that base again, including after an inttoptr truncation to P(a). Use PT2’s represented-address image construction at W bits (full-width arithmetic, no preserved high GEP bits), retaining original object and ROOT provenance. Keep modular displacement alternatives even outside the allocation, and additionally include (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤ when an image may leave it. Residue classes and `*` take the same finite-width image; an unrepresentable image retains (X, `*`) for each originating X plus (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤. Other integer operations on pointer atoms use that conservative image. Widening a pointer-derived integer by zext/sext uses that conservative image too (fixed reference fallback); ptrtoint zero-extension retains its base representation. Numerical uncertainty does not itself establish host escape (PT2). The rules apply also to constant expressions, before candidate/dispatch domains freeze; on finite `INT` sets, every LLVM integer operation, cast and `icmp` is evaluated elementwise; with x ⊤ and a constant m, `and x, m` gives the submasks of m and `urem x, m` gives {0, …, m−1} (each still subject to K); a range attribute or `!range` metadata removed by N3/N4 is not a bound on materialised execution and MUST NOT restrict any verdict, candidate set, descriptor shape or dispatch domain. Only bounds independently established by materialised instructions restrict the set; otherwise retain ⊤ and apply the ordinary operation rules, including finite masks/remainders |
1029:| PT6 | `load T, ptr p` | for each (X,o) ∈ AV(p): memory atoms carry their storage interval and representation width; copy a whole-value atom only for that identical interval with a compatible representation. A known immutable byte image decodes exactly the accessed bytes using DataLayout and T (including initialisers). For a partial, spanning or overlapping access whose result bits are not represented exactly, the non-handle value is ⊤, never the union of component INT values. X=EXT gives ⊤. A non-handle top flag on p contributes the same unknown-address load alternative even when explicit object offsets overlap no known slot; it MUST NOT be dropped. ROOT provenance remains explicit and §6.2's partial-handle restrictions still apply. For a possibly valid load wholly contained in a zero-filled initial extent, include the all-zero value of the load’s representation and width, unioned with values from every overlapping store or copy under these interval rules. PT8 propagates these initial byte possibilities when copying memory. Zero contributes no ROOT. Region inputs, replayed caller bytes and other nonzero-initialised contents retain their specified sources; this rule does not replace them with zeros |
1030:| PT7 | `store v, ptr p` | retain each written interval and representation width; partial writes overlapping a larger load participate in PT6's conservative fallback. AV(X, o + d_k) ⊇ AV(v_k) for each (X, o) ∈ AV(p) and each lane or field k at byte offset d_k |
1031:| PT8 | memory copy (`llvm.memcpy`/`memmove`/`memcpy.inline`, `__bennett_rt_memmove`) of (dst, src, len); memory fill (`llvm.memset`, `__bennett_rt_memset`) of (dst, v, len) | copy: propagate zero-byte initial images over the copied byte ranges, including their symbolic extents; for each slot (Y, s) of each (Y, o_s) ∈ AV(src) and (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst): when offsets and len are constants and the atom's entire storage interval [s, s + size(atom)) lies in the copied range, copy its atom and representation width to (Z, o_d + s − o_s). Every partially overlapping atom instead contributes non-handle ⊤ and explicit ROOT provenance to the affected destination interval (unknown offsets/len use `*`); do not copy the whole component INT. Fill: every slot in [o_d, o_d + len) of each (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst) (all of Z when an offset or len is not constant) gets the `INT` atoms of the byte v repeated to the slot's width (⊤ when v is not constant) |
1032:| PT9 | direct call of G, or indirect call with candidate G (§14.2) | AV(parameter i of G) ⊇ AV(argument i); AV(result) ⊇ the union of AV over G's `ret` operands; `byval` arguments copy the pointee's slots into the callee's copy object |
1033:| PT10 | `insertelement`/`extractelement`/`shufflevector`/`insertvalue`/`extractvalue` | extraction/insertion use N15’s total unsigned index semantics. Constant in-range extraction copies its lane; constant out-of-range extraction gives the element type’s zero. Dynamic extraction unions every source lane selected by a represented index, plus zero iff the index set includes an unsigned value >= L (including top when 2^K > L). No zero is added if every represented index is in range; zero adds no ROOT. In-range insertion replaces the selected lane; out-of-range insertion preserves the vector. Dynamic insertion includes each old lane unless it is certainly replaced, and the inserted value whenever that lane can be selected. Valid shuffle indices and aggregate paths move atoms unchanged (handle-table reads, audit B-23) |
1034:| PT11 | a parameter of a function with a host caller, a device entry, or a function whose address host code uses | AV ⊇ {(EXT, `*`)} for `ptr` parameters, ⊤ for integer parameters, plus the atoms of the device-side arguments by PT9; for a pointer parameter i of a device entry E, also the candidate atom ROOT(E, i) of §6.6 |
1035:| PT12 | a `qalloc`/`qborrow` call; a call of a function with no definition that resolves to a host call (§4.5) or is trapped; an `atomicrmw`/`cmpxchg` (before N9) | `qalloc`/`qborrow`: AV ∋ ROOT(site) (§6.5). An undefined callee: the result is ⊤ and every slot reachable from its pointer arguments gets ⊤. Before domains freeze, PT12 MUST apply PT6 to the old value, the PT5 transfers of the full N9 update, and PT7 to the actual stored value, with cmpxchg returning its old-value and success fields and storing the selected new-or-old value. [deps: DLT-5.2-P1, DLT-5.2-P6, DLT-5.2-P7, DLT-5.2-P8, DLT-5.2-P13, DLT-12.2-P11, ISA-4.1, ISA-4.2] |
1042:**PT12 N9 update witnesses (DB/DN/DS; non-normative).** The global starts at 1. Xor by 2 alternates 1 and 3 in the flow-insensitive least fixpoint; a single execution allocates width 3. Cmpxchg against 1 first succeeds and writes 3; subsequent abstract iterations include failure on old 3. Against 9 it always fails and leaves 1. Native LLVM 18/23 materialised O0/O3 checks old/success, actual width, clean free, liveness and code 0.

6797:## 13. Floating point (arith contract)
6803:### 13.1 Soft-float rewrite (rules N19, N32)
6842:For each such name fmt and each B of §13.1, N32 MUST emit `iN @__bennett_sf_convert_to_<fmt>_B(C_B, i32 R, i1 Sat)` for to-arbitrary (format MDString removed, rounding encoded by §13.5, immediate saturation retained), and `C_B @__bennett_sf_convert_from_<fmt>_B(iN)` for from-arbitrary; fmt is spelled exactly as its case-sensitive MDString, N is its listed bit width, and results are integer bits / bitcast B respectively. Finite conversions use the named format's rounding, saturation and nonfinite rules with canonical NaNs; N1 defines a source-poison conversion result as zero bits, including unsaturated out-of-range FP4/FP6 inputs. A missing APFloat lowering is an implementation obligation, never permission to reject a valid named format or emit an unresolved numerical placeholder.
6844:**Static instantiation request v1.** The driver MUST collect required concrete arith instances before the final link and supply a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly `schema: "bennett-arith-instantiations-1"`, `llvm_major` (integer), `target_triple` (string), `data_layout` (LLVM string), and `entries` (array); a conversion entry has exactly `op: "convert_to" | "convert_from"`, `format` (the MDString above), and `base` (B suffix), and a powi entry has exactly `op: "powi"`, `base`, and `exponent_bits` (integer K, 1..8388608). A powi entry requests both `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>(C_B, iK)` and `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>_rm(C_B, iK, i32 R)` (the suffix `_rm` is last); a conversion entry requests its exact signature above. Duplicate entries denote one instance; canonical request bytes are JCS with entries sorted by (op, base, format or empty string, exponent_bits or zero).
6848:**Wide integer sides (M>128).** The preceding i128 conversion rows apply only
6893:**PPCDoubleDouble priority and rounding.** The reference to the LLVM major's APFloat `PPCDoubleDouble` names its two-binary64 value representation and operation conventions, not permission to violate this section's explicit numerical guarantees: implementations MUST preserve the exact real value high + low for conversions and the correctly-rounded requirements for `sqrt`, `fma` and rounding-to-integral operations, and MUST NOT substitute the legacy consecutive-106-bit fallback when it changes such a result.
6897:Cross-format conversion MUST use the exact sum of both components, be exact whenever that sum is representable in the destination, and otherwise round once to the destination format under the requested mode; it MUST NOT drop a representable low component, saturate an in-range positive total because its low component is negative, or introduce intermediate-conversion flags. For example, high = 1 and low = 2^-60 convert to x86_fp80 as exactly 1 + 2^-60, not 1. The `fpext` exactness statement describes true numerical widening; a cross-format pair whose value sets are not nested still uses this conversion rule, regardless of equal storage width.
6899:Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part. Other PPC operations retain their named major-specific APFloat semantics subject to the explicit canonical-NaN rules above.

6921:C_B and suffix are exactly §13.1's table. No conversion through D is emitted.
6931:named operation numerically in B, using the same deterministic, versioned
6953:runtimes/arith versions. No correct-rounding claim is required; bit-exact
6961:For `_rm` carrier transcendentals, implementations MUST choose a result within the same envelope on the requested side of the exact result for directed modes, use nearest-side selection for the two nearest modes, and raise IEEE invalid for a domain error or signaling NaN, divbyzero for a pole, overflow when the correctly rounded mathematical result overflows B, underflow when that result is tiny after rounding and inexact, and inexact iff the returned finite value differs from the exact mathematical result; approximation work alone MUST NOT set a flag on an exactly representable mathematical result. For nearest-side selection, compare the distances to the two B neighbours bracketing the exact result, with ties to even or away as requested; this constrained case is correctly rounded. Unconstrained carrier operations retain the one-ulp allowance. Flags for modf/powi follow their exact split/sequenced arithmetic semantics. The native-format musl libm accuracy policy remains the recorded, versioned musl implementation; the quantitative carrier envelope applies to every own-format carrier route.

8711:**`indirectbr-two-blocks`.** The two address-taken blocks have values B(f)=4096 and B(f)+1=4097. The address object has two bytes. An IN(64,zext) pointer selects the first or second block and returns 7 or 9. Any other address reaches fault 4. The unused ptrtoint of the block object keeps its address explicitly in the reference closure. Success counts are wrapper 2 + address rows 2 + compare path 3 or 6 + return 2: 9 and 12 rows. No-match executes only the wrapper call, address rows 2, compares 6 and fault 1, for 10 rows; wrapper retval never executes.

10317:### 18.3 Marker rules
10327:| `qborrow(cls, n)` | as `qalloc` | inside a generation compiled as an ISA action, ISA `qborrow`/`qreturn` with borrow-frame verification (ISA §5.6); otherwise as `qalloc` (a correct user of borrowed wires also works on clean wires; ISA-9, §25.3). 0.1's `T-BORROW` is deleted |
10333:| `measure(h, i)` | as `gate`; no recorder active unless deferred (§7.5a) (`T-MEASURE`) | projective measurement of `h.i` (ISA §14.2); outcome m with its Born probability; the wire stays live in state m (ISA: copy h.i onto a fresh one-wire non-view register and measure that copy; h is never whole-register measured, ISA-6 not a gap); returns m |

11869:## Appendix A. Changes from the ratified outline
11876:| A1 | §8.6: k + 2 calls, cleaning each slice's fault bundle by `qxor` from a copy computed by a separate variant W_c | 2k − 1 calls: a copy-out pair per extra slice, issued as CC-level region calls (§8.6) | the outline's `qxor` cleanup is neither `reverse` nor `recompute` of the bundle's own computing sequence (ISA §6.2, §6.4), so as an action it would be ill-formed; the copy-out pair cleans its temporaries by the oracle's involution (ISA §5.2), checked by `qreg.free` (ISA §3.3) |
11888:| A13 | §3.8: a `@cfunction` constant "resolves whenever the body is in the link" | it is dispatched by value and traps unless it equals some A(F) (§3.8, §5.2) | A(F) is fixed by the comparison link after the analysis, and a Julia-process address never equals it; Bennett.jl's `ptr @f` route keeps the capability (audit B-14) |
11889:| A14 | §7.2: thunk resolution as 0.1 C1 (a cycle is `BD-E-REGION-THUNK`) | a thunk on a cycle resolves to itself; the code is deleted | a rejection with no NS clause behind it |
11893:| A18 | §16.4: compare-tree blocks | names `^b<i>.s<j>` in creation order and `^b<i>.q<q>c<k>`/`d`/`r` for indirect calls | deterministic text and hashes |
11895:| A20 | §25: wording items | adds certificate fields (build record, budget table, deferred measurement) and ISA §12.2 domains with a fixed classical region | NS §0, §5.1 require them in the certificate; NS §8 rung 4(a) is such a domain |

The heap companion uses a zero-filled calloc allocation-site object with the

whose initial value is 1: zero must come from the source, not the destination.

**PT3 geometry-changing bitcast witnesses (DB/DN/DS).** On this little-endian
layout, <1,1> packs to 1 | (1 << 8) = 257, and <0,1> packs to 256.
Each result is one scalar INT atom, never two source-lane atoms. The inverse
probe splits 769 = 1 + (3 << 8) into <1,3>, then selects 3. All three
are accepted with finite dispatch; no BLK-1 applies. The state checks verify
source O0, instcombine and virtual-N21 materialisation on LLVM 18/23, then
execute the source and materialised forms with native width-domain guards at
O0/O3. The first flips wire 256 twice; every allocation frees cleanly, code 0.

```llvm example=pt3-pack257 verdict=accept
target datalayout = "e-p:64:64"
define void @top() {
 %v0 = insertelement <2 x i8> zeroinitializer, i8 1, i32 0
 %v1 = insertelement <2 x i8> %v0, i8 1, i32 1
 %bits = bitcast <2 x i8> %v1 to i16
 %n = zext i16 %bits to i64
 %h = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 %n)
 call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %h, i64 256)
 call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %h, i64 256)
 call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h)
 ret void
}
```

```text state=pt3-pack257 at=0
{"domains":{"%bits":[257],"%n":[257]},"value_set":[257],"widths":[257],"gates":2,"index":256,"frees":1,"final_bit":0,"live_after":0,"code":0}
```

```llvm example=pt3-pack256 verdict=accept
target datalayout = "e-p:64:64-i64:64-n8:16:32:64"
declare ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64, i64)
declare void @__bennett_qfree(ptr)

 %a = insertelement <2 x i8> zeroinitializer, i8 1, i32 1

 %width = zext i16 %b to i64
 %h = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 %width)
```

**PT10 totalised lane domain.** The dynamic extraction from <1,3>
has domain {0,1,3}, so adding one gives finite width dispatch {1,2,4}.
Indices 0,1,2,-1 allocate 2,4,1,1 wires and free cleanly; no precondition
excludes the out-of-range host inputs.

```llvm example=pt10-lane-zero verdict=accept
define void @top(i32 %index) {
 %v0 = insertelement <2 x i64> zeroinitializer, i64 1, i32 0
 %v1 = insertelement <2 x i64> %v0, i64 3, i32 1
 %lane = extractelement <2 x i64> %v1, i32 %index
 %frozen = freeze i64 %lane
 %width = add i64 %frozen, 1
 %h = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 %width)
 call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h)
 ret void
}
```

```text state=pt10-lane-zero at=0
{"lane_set":[0,1,3],"value_set":[1,2,4],"inputs":[0,1,2,-1],"widths":[2,4,1,1],"live_after":0,"trap":0}
```

**Pointer-integer wrap witnesses.** Two i64 additions of −2^63 have net
zero displacement modulo 2^64, so p=@tab and f=@body. Modular displacement
alternatives are retained alongside unknown-address alternatives, never lost at
an intermediate out-of-object value. A ptrtoint to i128 followed by +2^64 and
inttoptr to a 64-bit pointer likewise returns @tab. The third witness computes
the address of a different allocation: unknown-address loading must retain its
possible function value. With adequate budgets dispatch succeeds exactly once.

```llvm example=pt5-int-wrap verdict=accept candidates=body
target datalayout = "e-p:64:64"
@tab = internal constant [1 x ptr] [ptr @body]
declare void @__bennett_adj(ptr, ptr, ptr)
define internal void @body(ptr %e) {
 ret void
}
define void @top() {
 %a = ptrtoint ptr @tab to i64
 %b = add i64 %a, -9223372036854775808
 %c = add i64 %b, -9223372036854775808
 %p = inttoptr i64 %c to ptr
 %f = load ptr, ptr %p, align 8
 call void @__bennett_adj(ptr %f, ptr null, ptr null)
 ret void
}
```

```text state=pt6-unknown-address at=0
{"candidates":["body"],"calls":1,"trap":0}
```

**PT6 byte reconstruction witnesses.** Immutable bytes [1,1] decode as
1+256=257 under e-p:64:64, never the union {1}. A two-byte subrange of a
mutable four-byte store of 66049 (bytes 01 02 01 00) concretely gives 513,
but has no exact whole-interval atom: its fixed abstract result is TOP, not {66049}.
The fallback executes width 513 without claiming a finite reference dispatch set.

```llvm example=pt6-byte-width verdict=accept
target datalayout = "e-p:64:64"
@width = internal constant [2 x i8] [i8 1, i8 1]
define void @top() {
  %w = load i16, ptr @width, align 1
  %n = zext i16 %w to i64
  %h = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 %n)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %h, i64 256)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %h, i64 256)
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h)
  ret void
}
```

```text state=pt6-byte-width
{"value_set":[257],"width":257,"wire":256,"live_after":0,"trap":0}
```

```llvm example=pt6-subrange verdict=blocked:BD-B-ISA4
target datalayout = "e-p:64:64"
define void @top() {
  %p = alloca i32
  store i32 66049, ptr %p
  %w = load i16, ptr %p, align 1
  %n = zext i16 %w to i64
  %h = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 %n)
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h)
  ret void
```

```text state=pt6-subrange
{"value_set":"TOP","width":513,"live_after":0,"trap":0}
```

A constant-byte fill carries its complete filled interval and byte value; a load
contained in that interval decodes the repeated byte at the load's width. Other
overlapping writes still participate in PT6 (a fill does not erase them).
PT8 copies preserve storage intervals/representation widths only for fully copied
atoms. Partial copies and fills add overlapping affected intervals; PT6 then returns
⊤ for any load whose whole bits are not represented exactly. These are fixed
reference rules, not optional precision improvements.

Handle-array propagation MUST retain field intervals and finite-width address images so that a disjoint integer counter is not handle-derived, without treating an array type as a proof that indices stay in bounds. [deps: DLT-5.1-P7, DLT-5.2-P3, DLT-5.2-P7, DLT-5.2-P8, ISA-4.1, ISA-4.2]

**Compatible allocation/collection witness (non-normative).** A defined wrapper
returns an attributed fresh allocation whose name is not a built-in allocator.
A two-level heap header carries a handle through a shared global; a disjoint
counter remains ordinary integer data. Two indirect candidates return different
ROOTs. An i1 vector index selects exactly one live lane without an out-of-range
zero alternative. Both inputs execute four X gates, two clean frees and code0;
LLVM18/23 source and materialised O0/O3 execute independently of the analysis.

```llvm example=compatible-allocator-graph verdict=accept callees=first,second
@shared = internal global { ptr, i64 } zeroinitializer
@observed_old = global i64 0
@observed_success = global i64 0
declare ptr @custom_alloc(i64) allocsize(0)
declare void @free(ptr)
define internal ptr @make_node(ptr %h) {
  %node = call ptr @custom_alloc(i64 16)
  store ptr %h, ptr %node
  %link = getelementptr i8, ptr %node, i64 8
  store ptr null, ptr %link
  ret ptr %node
}
define internal ptr @first(ptr %a, ptr %b) {
  ret ptr %a
}
define internal ptr @second(ptr %a, ptr %b) {
  ret ptr %b
}
define void @host_count() {
  %counter = getelementptr { ptr, i64 }, ptr @shared, i64 0, i32 1
  store i64 9, ptr %counter
  ret void
}
define void @top(i1 %choose) {
  %a = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 1)
  %b = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 1)
  %node = call ptr @make_node(ptr %a)
  %header = call ptr @custom_alloc(i64 8)
  store ptr %node, ptr %header
  store ptr %header, ptr @shared
  %loaded_header = load ptr, ptr @shared
  %loaded_node = load ptr, ptr %loaded_header
  %from_node = load ptr, ptr %loaded_node
  %f = select i1 %choose, ptr @first, ptr @second
  %from_callback = call ptr %f(ptr %from_node, ptr %b)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %from_callback, i64 0)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %from_callback, i64 0)
  %v0 = insertelement <2 x ptr> zeroinitializer, ptr %a, i32 0
  %v1 = insertelement <2 x ptr> %v0, ptr %b, i32 1
  %lane = extractelement <2 x ptr> %v1, i1 %choose
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %lane, i64 0)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %lane, i64 0)
  call void @host_count()
  %counter = getelementptr { ptr, i64 }, ptr @shared, i64 0, i32 1
  %count = load i64, ptr %counter
  store i64 %count, ptr @observed_old
  call void @free(ptr %node)
  call void @free(ptr %header)
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %a)
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %b)
  ret void
}
```

```text state=compatible-allocator-graph at=return
{"roots":{"%from_node":["top%a"],"%from_callback":["top%a","top%b"],"%lane":["top%a","top%b"]},"counter_roots":[],"native":[[9,2,4,2,0,0],[9,2,4,2,0,0]]}
```

### 5.3 Value sets

The **value set** VS(v) of an integer or pointer operand is the set of its `INT` and `FN` atoms
(pointer atoms denote addresses that are not compile-time constants and make VS(v) = ⊤ for
dispatch purposes), or ⊤. A value set is a precision tool only:

- an operand with a finite VS is dispatched over it (§3.4, §14.2);
- an operand with VS = ⊤ is dispatched over its type's whole valid domain where that domain is
  finite: operation codes and classes (§3.4), binding kinds (§8.4), the function addresses of a
  type (§7.2, §14.2);
- otherwise it is a blocker case of §25.1: widths (BLK-1), budgets computed on the device
  (BLK-2), parameters of in-place pairs (§8.7).

### 5.4 Uses

| Result | Read by |
|---|---|
| `FN` atoms of region operands | region candidate sets (§7.2) |
| `FN` atoms of indirect callees | indirect-call candidate sets (§14.2) |
| `ROOT` atoms | handle provenance (§6.2), handle containment (§6.4), static register files (§6.5) |
| `INT` atoms of descriptor and budget fields | descriptor shapes (§8.4), budget pre-minting (§9.5) |
| slots stored under control | the may-Co analysis and arena placement (§10, TDL-2, TDL-4) |
| objects loaded by L code | the oracle read set R and entry-state transfer (§8.5) |
| `INT` atoms of `op`, `cls`, `n` | dispatch domains (§3.4) |

```llvm example=pt-variable-index verdict=accept
%struct.tab = type { [4 x ptr], i64 }

define void @t(i64 %i) {
  %s = alloca %struct.tab
  %h0 = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 1)
  store ptr %h0, ptr %s
  %pn = getelementptr %struct.tab, ptr %s, i64 0, i32 1
  store i64 3, ptr %pn
  %k = and i64 %i, 3
  %pk = getelementptr %struct.tab, ptr %s, i64 0, i32 0, i64 %k
  %hk = load ptr, ptr %pk
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %hk, i64 0)
  %n = load i64, ptr %pn
  %n2 = add i64 %n, 1
  store i64 %n2, ptr %pn
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h0)
  ret void
}
```

*(non-normative)* In `pt-variable-index`, `%pk` has the offset class (0 mod 8)[0, 32) in `%s`
(the `[4 x ptr]` array), so the load `%hk` reads the slots 0, 8, 16, 24: AV(`%hk`) =
{ROOT(`%h0`)}. The count field is at the exact offset 32, outside the class, so AV(`%n`) holds
INT(3) and, through the store of `%n2`, INT(4), INT(5), … until it exceeds K and becomes ⊤; it
holds no `ROOT` atom, and the arithmetic on it is legal (audit B-27: 0.1 gave the load offset `*`
and rejected this program).

**Finite-width and materialised-domain witnesses (round 6).** Under I=32,
2^32 truncates to zero, so the table load yields FN(body) and dispatch succeeds.
For the ranged inputs, N3/N4 erase the annotations: `n & 3` gives {0,1,2,3}
and `| 1` gives {1,3}. At n=2 width 3 admits both X gates on wire 2 and a clean
free. No new input precondition or fault is introduced.

```llvm example=pt2-wrap verdict=accept
target datalayout = "e-p:64:64:64:32"
@tab = internal constant [1 x ptr] [ptr @body]
declare void @__bennett_adj(ptr, ptr, ptr)
define internal void @body(ptr %e) {
 ret void
}
define void @top() {
 %p = getelementptr i8, ptr @tab, i64 4294967296
 %f = load ptr, ptr %p, align 8
 call void @__bennett_adj(ptr %f, ptr null, ptr null)
 ret void
}
```

```text state=pt2-wrap
{"pointer_offset":0,"candidates":["body"],"calls":1,"trap":0}
```

```llvm example=pt5-range verdict=accept majors=23
declare ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64, i64)
declare void @__bennett_gate(i32, ptr, i64)
declare void @__bennett_qfree(ptr)
define void @top(i64 range(i64 1, 2) %n) {
  %m = and i64 %n, 3
  %w = or i64 %m, 1
  %h = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 %w)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %h, i64 2)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %h, i64 2)
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h)
  ret void
}
```

```text state=pt5-range
{"value_set":[1,3],"input":2,"width":3,"live_after":0,"trap":0}
```

```llvm example=pt5-range-load verdict=accept
declare ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64, i64)
declare void @__bennett_gate(i32, ptr, i64)
declare void @__bennett_qfree(ptr)
define void @top(ptr %p) {
  %n = load i64, ptr %p, !range !0
  %m = and i64 %n, 3
  %w = or i64 %m, 1
  %h = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 %w)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %h, i64 2)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %h, i64 2)
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h)
  ret void
}
!0 = !{i64 1, i64 2}
```
