## 6. Handles (handles 0.3)

**Reference semantics.** The region function runs on a **private copy of the caller's whole
classical memory at the call**, with the bound inputs; its writes are discarded except through
`OUT`, `tgt` and the bound device outputs (NS §4.2: "every other write being restored
workspace"). Constant globals, and mutable globals that only the oracle's own L closure writes,
equal their initialisers at every call.

**Realisation on ISA 0.9.** Let R be the abstract objects the oracle closure may load (§5.4),
minus constant globals and mutable globals written by no function outside the L closure.
Translate source read-set objects after normalisation to runtime backing allocations. If
R includes a caller alloca/frame, include its live shadow-stack backing storage and metadata,
bytes and addresses. If reachability is uncertain, include all live caller heap/stack backing
allocations. Whenever the L closure uses the shadow stack, add its state globals to G and
its block to Hb with current base, size and stack pointer, even without a source heap object.
Include the logical frame/allocation table of §17.3, dynamic extents, liveness and
save/restore state in G whenever stack access or stack operations can execute. Replay
these bytes before private allocations/accesses and let ss_init adopt them; a live
backing entry alone does not recreate live program frames. All such metadata mutations
invalidate snapshot reuse independently of the byte read set.
Allocator metadata is an independent entry-state dependency: allocation, deallocation,
allocation-validity checks and observation of allocation-derived addresses read it even
without an LLVM load. Replay the complete caller allocation history (sizes and live bits)
whenever any can execute, including when the byte read set R is empty. Bytes are included
only for byte dependencies; the sound default is the complete table, with a smaller proven
dependency optional. Only when both byte and allocator-state dependencies are absent may
transfer be omitted and globals start at their images
(ISA §3.7), as in rung 4 route A (SQLite §5.2(i)). After replay heap_used, atop and stack-state
values equal the caller's call-entry values.
Otherwise the plugin emits an **entry-state transfer**:

1. **Snapshot.** At the call the caller's tier forms the byte string
   S = k ‖ (size_i ‖ live_i)_{i<k} ‖ G ‖ Hb, where k is an 8-byte little-endian count of heap allocations in the
   caller's allocation table (in allocation order, ISA §7.1), `size_i` and `live_i` are 8-byte
   little-endian fields (`live_i` is 0 or 1), G is the bytes of the mutable globals in R in `layout.globals` order,
   and Hb is the bytes of every live heap allocation in allocation order if R contains a heap
   object or shadow-stack backing allocation (else empty); each live Hb segment has exactly size_i bytes, even for size_i=0. S is zero-padded to 2^A_s bytes, where A_s is the smallest exponent with
   2^A_s ≥ 8 + 16·N_alloc + Σ|G| + `heap_limit_max` (N_alloc the allocation-table capacity of
   the layout, §19.5); A_s is static.
   The snapshot bytes S are assembled in a plugin-added unnamed CC global of 2^A_s bytes,
   zero-filled before every build; thus all 16-byte leaf loads, including padding, are valid.
2. **Register.** The CC tier builds a register of 8·2^A_s wires holding S with a **concat
   tree** of A_s − 3 static-width CC functions `B_j` (j = 4, …, A_s): `B_4(p)` executes
   `qreg.new any 128`, loads the 16 bytes at p as two `i64` values and makes the `oracle_call`
   of the runtime oracle `@xor128` (§16.5) that XORs them into the register (with a token minted
   at launch for budget (6, 0, 0, 0) and a fresh 4-wire fault register, which stays clean and is
   freed); `B_j(p)` (j > 4)
   returns `qreg.concat B_{j−1}(p) B_{j−1}(p + 2^{j−1})`. Every type is static and the cost is
   2^(A_s−4) region calls. The register is bound as an extra `in` region `s0:region[A_s,8]`.
3. **Replay.** The wrapper prologue (§16.2) calls the runtimes function
   `__bennett_rt_entry_replay(s0)`, which for i < k executes `__bennett_nz_alloc(size_i)` (ISA
   `lift.alloc`) and, if `live_i` = 0, `__bennett_nz_free` of the result. ISA §4.2 `lift.alloc`
   and ISA §7.1 `alloc` share the rule "bump + max(n, 1) from `heap_base`", so the replay
   reproduces the caller's heap addresses exactly (audit O-07). It then copies G into the
   globals (by the driver-generated table `__bennett_rt_entry_table` of their addresses and
   sizes) and Hb into the replayed live allocations.
4. **Release.** The snapshot register holds the basis state |S⟩ and is unentangled after every
   oracle call (an `in` region is unchanged on every basis configuration, ISA §5.2). The caller
   keeps it for later calls only while its dependent bytes and allocator metadata are unchanged. Relevant alloc/free
   and shadow-stack push/pop/restore invalidate it as well as stores to R,
   and releases it by unpreparation: retain the classical S for the register's lifetime,
   take successive slice views of at most 128 bits, invoke the same @xor128 preparation
   oracle with the original two i64 arguments on each 128-bit chunk (one view per call),
   then qreg.free the now-clean whole register. A_s ≥ 4, so every leaf is exactly 128 bits.
   No whole-register measurement, view measurement or un-concat operation is needed.

The replay's rows, allocations and calls are part of the reference body and count against the
budget (§9.4). Runtime oracle entry state MUST retain caller bytes and allocator replay in native address order before any wrapper-private allocation, and R callers MUST use reversible register preparation and release for that same snapshot construction. [deps: DLT-8.5, DLT-16.2-P20, ISA-7.1, ISA-7.2, ISA-3.3-P18, ISA-7.4] An R caller executes steps 1, 2 and 4 in R (ISA K2) and passes tokens it holds or mints (§9.5). **Build-time snapshots** (rung 7, §19.6) rewrite the initialisers of
the mutable globals to snapshot values at link time, so byte transfer may be empty; allocator dependencies still require replay.
Efficiency: G-12 (§25.3).

### 8.6 Oracle invocations and wide outputs
### 19.1 Pipeline position and bitcode sources

Front ends emit bitcode with their stock pre-link pipelines; no Bennett code runs before the final
link (Enzyme PR #3211: "forgetting it is a loud failure"), except in the explicit closed-unit mode
(§19.7). The driver links all bitcode inputs (§19.8), links `bennett-runtimes` and `bennett-arith`
bitcode of the link's major M, runs the post-link pipeline (`lto<O3>` by default, or any envelope
pipeline of §21.1 the user selects), then runs the plugin on the whole program (NS §3.3). With
`opt`:

```
opt -load-pass-plugin=libbennett-plugin-<M>.so -passes='lto<O3>,bennett' -bennett-dialect=0.3 …
```

The plugin runs on one merged module (full LTO; §24 D23). It registers one module pass, `bennett`,
and MAY register a full-LTO last extension point for use from a linker plugin; it MUST do nothing
in a pre-link phase. **Bitcode sources** (NS §3.3): Rust `std` from the bitcode embedded in rlibs
(`.llvmbc`) where present and readable by §19.8, else `-Zbuild-std`; the Julia runtime by the
runtimes' shim bitcode; every remaining call without bitcode is a `trapped` coverage line (§4.5).

### 19.2 Mode `--bennett-tier=reversible --bennett-entry=<sym>`

The whole program is the device program in tier R (NS §4.2: "entered through
`__bennett_reversible` or a driver mode"; rungs 3, 5, 6):

- a CC **launch stub** fetches the argv and envp bytes with `hostcall bennett.argv`, initialises
  the shadow stack (§17.3), calls the R entry `<sym>` with (argc, argv, envp), catches the exit
  pseudo-exception (§14.7) and returns the status;
- effects are the ISA device models (§11.2): console, clock, entropy (the launch's entropy tape),
  block with `block_image` from `--bennett-disk=<file>`;
- un-run is `bennett_reverse` (ISA §10): whole-run reversal restores the entry state; tape replay is
  deterministic from the tape and the entropy. Whole-program R mode MUST retain the unmodified entry route and effect tape, and MUST report terminal faults and reverse eligible completed or failed runs according to ISA 0.9. [deps: DLT-19.2, DLT-14.7, DLT-18.6, ISA-4.9-P1, ISA-7.4, ISA-10]
- a `measure` reachable in the program is `BD-E-REVERSIBLE-MEASURE` (§7.5b).

**Process context.** Before entering `<sym>`, the driver/runtimes preparation MUST establish a static single-thread musl process context with the supplied argc/argv/envp bytes, a null-terminated argv and envp, `environ`, program name, initial fenv and the libc/stdio state needed by the linked program; comparison-only Linux `_start` is permitted, but Linux ELF crt/TLS/auxv entry is not required in device tiers.

For a C `main` entry, the runtimes entry wrapper MUST treat normal return as `exit(status)`, running registered exit handlers and flushing stdio exactly once under §14.7 before the launch stub catches the exit pseudo-exception; explicit `exit` follows that same path, and `_Exit` bypasses handlers/flush as §14.7 specifies.

A terminal R fault (§17.6) is none of these paths: exit handlers and stdio flush do not run, the launch stub receives no status, and the launch result carries the code (ISA §4.9, §10). The effect tape holds exactly the effects before the fault. Reversal of that `FAILED` run removes them (ISA K6).

### 19.3 Mode `--bennett-oracle=<sym>` ("oracle from program")

A convenience: the driver synthesises the translation unit a quantum programmer would write
(§16.7): a CC function that builds a descriptor binding `<sym>`'s parameters by
`--bennett-bind=<param>:<kind>:<width>[:<value>]` (kind `in`, `arg`, `mem_in`, `out`), device
bindings by `--bennett-dev=<model>:<width>[:<value>]`, and a payload: the return value, the exit
status caught by the wrapper (§14.7), or the first L bytes of console output
(`--bennett-payload=return|exit|console:<L>`), and applies the oracle once to registers it
allocates, as a device entry the host calls with those registers. The normative route is the
separate translation unit with marker calls naming the unmodified function (rungs 4, 7, 8).

### 19.4 Placement: host vs device CC

H functions stay native unless they share **pointer-rich** memory with device code: a pointer
argument of a device-entry call, or a global accessed by both, whose pointee may contain pointers
that device code reads (§5). Such H functions are **promoted** to CC variants, transitively up to
their roots (in the limit `main`, and then H is the system-call proxy only: `hostcall
bennett.syscall`). Results are equal by the materialised semantics; ISA-11 would let them stay
native (an efficiency and placement item, §25.3; outline E11).

### 19.5 Layout: the comparison link

The driver links the materialised module natively (§17.7) with a linker script that fixes the
addresses: non-PIE; identical-code folding and constant merging off. The link map gives A(F)
for every function and the base of every program global. The following is the **single
range-order and non-overlap rule** for §6.5, TDL-4 and §16.3. `align_up(x,a)` is the least
multiple of positive a at or above x; all arithmetic below is mathematical, without wrapping.
All intervals are half-open.

1. Reserve the null region [0, `null_size`), default `null_size` = 4096. Lay out ordinary
   `.rodata`, `.data`, `.bss`, in that order, from `data_base` = 4096, with each object's
   target alignment. These include the arena's `__bennett_rt_shadow` when needed and exclude
   `__bennett_rt_qtab`. May-Co globals are relocated into the arena in both builds (TDL-4);
   they have no second classical global or image (§16.3). Let `data_end` be the end of
   ordinary data (or `data_base` if empty).
2. Set `heap_base = align_up(data_end,4096)` and `heap_limit_max` to the largest frozen
   `heap` capacity of the budget table (§9.2), including shadow-stack allocations. Reserve
   [heap_base, `heap_end`), where `heap_end = heap_base + heap_limit_max`.
3. If an arena exists, let its size 2^A be the smallest power of two covering the may-Co
   globals, their alignment padding and the coherent allocator's heap
   (`--bennett-arena-heap`, default the table's largest `heap`). Set
   `A_coh = align_up(heap_end,2^A)` and `coh_end = A_coh + 2^A`. Otherwise set
### 19.1 Pipeline position and bitcode sources

Front ends emit bitcode with their stock pre-link pipelines; no Bennett code runs before the final
link (Enzyme PR #3211: "forgetting it is a loud failure"), except in the explicit closed-unit mode
(§19.7). The driver links all bitcode inputs (§19.8), links `bennett-runtimes` and `bennett-arith`
bitcode of the link's major M, runs the post-link pipeline (`lto<O3>` by default, or any envelope
pipeline of §21.1 the user selects), then runs the plugin on the whole program (NS §3.3). With
`opt`:

```
opt -load-pass-plugin=libbennett-plugin-<M>.so -passes='lto<O3>,bennett' -bennett-dialect=0.2.12 …
```

The plugin runs on one merged module (full LTO; §24 D23). It registers one module pass, `bennett`,
and MAY register a full-LTO last extension point for use from a linker plugin; it MUST do nothing
in a pre-link phase. **Bitcode sources** (NS §3.3): Rust `std` from the bitcode embedded in rlibs
(`.llvmbc`) where present and readable by §19.8, else `-Zbuild-std`; the Julia runtime by the
runtimes' shim bitcode; every remaining call without bitcode is a `trapped` coverage line (§4.5).

### 19.2 Mode `--bennett-tier=reversible --bennett-entry=<sym>`

The whole program is the device program in tier R (NS §4.2: "entered through
`__bennett_reversible` or a driver mode"; rungs 3, 5, 6):

- a CC **launch stub** fetches the argv and envp bytes with `hostcall bennett.argv`, initialises
  the shadow stack (§17.3), calls the R entry `<sym>` with (argc, argv, envp), catches the exit
  pseudo-exception (§14.7) and returns the status;
- effects are the ISA device models (§11.2): console, clock, entropy (the launch's entropy tape),
  block with `block_image` from `--bennett-disk=<file>`;
- un-run is `bennett_reverse` (ISA §10): whole-run reversal restores the entry state; tape replay is
  deterministic from the tape and the entropy;
- a `measure` reachable in the program is `BD-E-REVERSIBLE-MEASURE` (§7.5b).

**Process context.** Before entering `<sym>`, the driver/runtimes preparation MUST establish a static single-thread musl process context with the supplied argc/argv/envp bytes, a null-terminated argv and envp, `environ`, program name, initial fenv and the libc/stdio state needed by the linked program; comparison-only Linux `_start` is permitted, but Linux ELF crt/TLS/auxv entry is not required in device tiers.

For a C `main` entry, the runtimes entry wrapper MUST treat normal return as `exit(status)`, running registered exit handlers and flushing stdio exactly once under §14.7 before the launch stub catches the exit pseudo-exception; explicit `exit` follows that same path, and `_Exit` bypasses handlers/flush as §14.7 specifies.

### 19.3 Mode `--bennett-oracle=<sym>` ("oracle from program")

A convenience: the driver synthesises the translation unit a quantum programmer would write
(§16.7): a CC function that builds a descriptor binding `<sym>`'s parameters by
`--bennett-bind=<param>:<kind>:<width>[:<value>]` (kind `in`, `arg`, `mem_in`, `out`), device
bindings by `--bennett-dev=<model>:<width>[:<value>]`, and a payload: the return value, the exit
status caught by the wrapper (§14.7), or the first L bytes of console output
(`--bennett-payload=return|exit|console:<L>`), and applies the oracle once to registers it
allocates, as a device entry the host calls with those registers. The normative route is the
separate translation unit with marker calls naming the unmodified function (rungs 4, 7, 8).

### 19.4 Placement: host vs device CC

H functions stay native unless they share **pointer-rich** memory with device code: a pointer
argument of a device-entry call, or a global accessed by both, whose pointee may contain pointers
that device code reads (§5). Such H functions are **promoted** to CC variants, transitively up to
their roots (in the limit `main`, and then H is the system-call proxy only: `hostcall
bennett.syscall`). Results are equal by the materialised semantics; ISA-11 would let them stay
native (an efficiency and placement item, §25.3; outline E11).

### 19.5 Layout: the comparison link

The driver links the materialised module natively (§17.7) with a linker script that fixes the
addresses: non-PIE; identical-code folding and constant merging off; the null region [0,
`null_size`) with `null_size` = 4096; the data sections (`.rodata`, `.data`, `.bss`, in that order)
from `data_base` = 4096; the text from `text_base` = 0x400000, or, when the device ranges below
(data, heap, arena, interface regions, computed first) end above 0x400000, from the first multiple
of 0x200000 at or after their end, so that the native comparison binary maps its code disjointly
from them; the defaults are options (`--bennett-layout-data-base`, `--bennett-layout-text-base`,
§24). The link map gives A(F) for
every function and the base of every global. The device `layout` (ISA §9.1) replicates it:

- `globals`: every global of the link at its link address with its image;
- `heap_base`: the first multiple of 4096 at or after the end of the data; `heap_limit_max`: the
  largest `heap` of the budget table (which covers the shadow stack, §9.2);
- the arena (TDL-4) at the first multiple of its size 2^A at or after the heap's end, with 2^A the
  smallest power of two covering the may-Co globals and the coherent allocator's heap (its size
  from `--bennett-arena-heap`, default the largest `heap` of the table); the arena's shadow
  `__bennett_rt_shadow` is a global of the data sections;
- interface regions per oracle above the arena (§16.3); H, A_tab and S_cells by §16.3;
- the production host image is linked with the same addresses for every function and global
  whose address may cross the boundary (§4.4).

*(non-normative)* With data low and text high, H covers the data, heap and regions; function
addresses are values only and never occupy device memory.

**Native layout ABI v1.** The driver MUST define exactly one external constant `@__bennett_rt_native_layout = constant { i64, i64, i64 } { i64 1, i64 heap_base, i64 heap_limit_max }, align 8` in comparison-only support, with fields at byte offsets 0, 8 and 16 in target little-endian order and values from this same resolved §19.5 layout; runtimes declares that object and validates the version/range before use, with no weak/default definition. The third field is a byte capacity, not an end address; it includes shadow-stack allocations under §9.2. This object has no relationship to blockaddress identity objects `__bennett_rt_blk.<f>`.

### 19.6 Build-time snapshots

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""PT6 loads update incrementally when an object's slots grow (bp-fj1); the result is the full pass's.

The hidden flag -bennett-analysis-check-delta recomputes every incremental load group in full and
aborts on any difference. This test runs it over every probe and fixture program and over
programs built to grow a loaded object round after round (stores of loaded values, partial
overlaps, strided stores, zero-filled heap images, a written constant image), and requires (a) no difference, (b) the
incremental path was actually taken where the program grows an object, and (c) the analysis
report is identical with and without the check.
"""
import argparse,json,re,subprocess,sys
from contracts import ROOT as R

def select_chain(base,offsets,ty='i8'):
 lines=[];prev=None
 for n,o in enumerate(offsets):
  lines.append(f' %q{n} = getelementptr {ty}, ptr {base}, i64 {o}')
  if prev is None: prev=f'%q{n}'
  else:
   lines.append(f' %r{n} = select i1 %c, ptr {prev}, ptr %q{n}');prev=f'%r{n}'
 return '\n'.join(lines),prev

def grow_global(slots=16):
 """A load of every slot feeds a store into one slot: the loaded set grows one value per round."""
 body,q=select_chain('@g',[8*n for n in range(slots)])
 stores='\n'.join(f' %s{n} = getelementptr i8, ptr @g, i64 {8*n}\n store i64 %a, ptr %s{n}' for n in (3,7,11))
 return f'''@g = internal global [{slots} x i64] zeroinitializer
define i64 @top(i1 %c) {{
{body}
 %v = load i64, ptr {q}
 %w = add i64 %v, 1
 %a = and i64 %w, 31
{stores}
 ret i64 %v
}}''','%v'

def grow_heap():
 """Zero-filled heap image, pointer stores into it, a partial (i32 of i64) load and a strided store."""
 body,q=select_chain('%b',[0,8,16,24,32])
 return f'''declare ptr @calloc(i64, i64)
@k = internal global i64 0
define i64 @top(i1 %c, i64 %i) {{
 %b = call ptr @calloc(i64 8, i64 8)
{body}
 %v = load i64, ptr {q}
 %h = load i32, ptr {q}
 %p = load ptr, ptr {q}
 %w = add i64 %v, 3
 %a = and i64 %w, 63
 %e = getelementptr i8, ptr %b, i64 16
 store i64 %a, ptr %e
 %f = getelementptr i8, ptr %b, i64 32
 store ptr @k, ptr %f
 %x = load i64, ptr %p
 %m = and i64 %i, 3
 %s = getelementptr i64, ptr %b, i64 %m
 store i64 %x, ptr %s
 %t = trunc i64 %a to i32
 %u = getelementptr i8, ptr %b, i64 40
 store i32 %t, ptr %u
 ret i64 %v
}}''','%v'

def grow_constant():
 """Stores reach a constant image through a merged pointer: its exact-byte loads lose and gain
 contributions as the written slots grow (the per-atom path), at byte and multi-byte widths."""
 body,q=select_chain('@s',[0,1,2,4,5])
 return f'''@s = internal constant [8 x i8] c"abcdefgh"
@h = internal global [8 x i8] zeroinitializer
define i32 @top(i1 %c, i1 %d) {{
{body}
 %b = load i8, ptr {q}
 %w = load i32, ptr {q}
 %x = load i16, ptr %q2
 %z = zext i8 %b to i32
 %y = add i32 %z, 1
 %t = trunc i32 %y to i8
 %k = and i8 %t, 7
 %dst = select i1 %d, ptr @h, ptr %q3
 store i8 %k, ptr %dst
 %e = getelementptr i8, ptr %dst, i64 1
 store i8 %b, ptr %e
 %r = add i32 %w, %y
 ret i32 %r
}}''','%w'

def run(m,src,check,tag):
 report=R/f'.tmp/load-delta/{m}/{tag}.json'
 argv=[f'/usr/lib/llvm-{m}/bin/opt','-load-pass-plugin='+str(R/f'build/libbennett-plugin-{m}.so'),'-passes=bennett','-bennett-dialect=0.2.10','-bennett-analysis-only','-bennett-report='+str(report),'-disable-output',str(src)]
 if check: argv.insert(4,'-bennett-analysis-check-delta')
 report.unlink(missing_ok=True)
 x=subprocess.run(argv,capture_output=True,text=True,timeout=600)
 data=report.read_text() if report.exists() else None
 return x.returncode,x.stderr,data

def main():
 ap=argparse.ArgumentParser();ap.add_argument('--major',type=int,required=True);a=ap.parse_args();m=a.major
 out=R/f'.tmp/load-delta/{m}';out.mkdir(parents=True,exist_ok=True)
 results=[]
 def check(name,ok,detail=''):
  results.append({'case':name,'ok':bool(ok),'detail':detail if not ok else ''})
 programs=[]
 for name,(text,_) in {'grow-global':grow_global(),'grow-heap':grow_heap(),'grow-constant':grow_constant()}.items():
  path=out/f'{name}.ll';path.write_text(text);programs.append((name,path,True))
 for path in sorted((R/'tests/probes').glob('*.ll'))+sorted((R/'tests/fixtures').glob('*.ll')):
  programs.append((path.parent.name+'-'+path.stem,path,False))
 for name,path,grows in programs:
  rc0,err0,data0=run(m,path,False,name+'-plain')
  rc1,err1,data1=run(m,path,True,name+'-checked')
  check(name+'-no-difference','incremental PT6 differs' not in err1,err1[-400:])
  check(name+'-same-exit',rc0==rc1,{'plain':rc0,'checked':rc1,'err':err1[-300:]})
  # Reports name no flag, so a checked run must report exactly what the plain run reports.
  check(name+'-same-report',data0==data1,'reports differ')
  checked=re.search(r'checked (\d+) incremental load groups',err1)
  if grows:
   check(name+'-incremental-taken',rc1==0 and checked and int(checked.group(1))>0,err1[-400:])
 summary={'major':m,'cases':len(results),'passed':sum(r['ok'] for r in results)}
 (out/'result.json').write_text(json.dumps(summary|{'results':results},indent=1))
 print(json.dumps(summary|{'failed':[r for r in results if not r['ok']][:10]}))
 sys.exit(0 if summary['passed']==summary['cases'] else 1)

def dialect_text():
 return subprocess.check_output(['git','-C',str(DIALECT),'show',PIN+':spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md'],text=True)
def drift():
 # An unaccepted working draft is not a change to the accepted contract.
 registry=tomllib.loads((DIALECT/'SPEC_VERSIONS.toml').read_text())
 pin=tomllib.loads((ROOT/'SPEC_VERSIONS.toml').read_text())
 return (registry['dialect']!='0.2.10' or pin['dialect']!='0.2.10'
         or pin['specs']['bennett-dialect-spec']!='0.2.10'
         or pin['isa']!='0.8' or pin['specs']['bennett-isa-spec']!='0.8'
         or hashlib.sha256(dialect_text().encode()).hexdigest()!='d4f3c1a88391cc147f8c8fa4f80f82852e3ebe6f880610fc16cd5151f33d7206'
         or pin['commits']['bennett-dialect-spec']!=PIN)

def arith_path(major):
 pin=tomllib.loads((ROOT/'SPEC_VERSIONS.toml').read_text())['arith']
 root=os.environ.get('BENNETT_ARITH_PINNED_ROOT')
 path=Path(root)/f'llvm{major}/bennett-arith.bc' if root else Path(pin[f'llvm{major}_path'])
 assert hashlib.sha256(path.read_bytes()).hexdigest()==pin[f'llvm{major}_sha256'], f'LLVM {major}: arith pin mismatch: {path}'
 return path

def arith_manifest(major):
 # The bitcode digest is checked by arith_path; metadata comes from that same
 # immutable publication, even when only the .bc files have been relocated.

std::set<Function *> Analysis::candidates(Value *v, FunctionType *type) {
  AV a = get(v);
  std::set<Function *> r;
  for (auto &x : a.atoms)
    if (x.kind == Atom::Fn)
      if (auto *f = resolve(const_cast<Value *>(x.object)))
        r.insert(f);
  bool top = a.top;
  for (auto &x : a.atoms)
    top |= x.kind == Atom::Ptr;
  if (top) {
    if(currentStep>=0) {
      // The module is frozen while solving; use lists need not be rescanned per site.
      if(!addressTakenKnown) {
        addressTakenKnown=true;
        for(Function &f:M)if(f.hasAddressTaken() && !markers().count(f.getName().str()))addressTaken.push_back(&f);
      }
      for(auto *f:addressTaken)if(!type || f->getFunctionType()==type)r.insert(resolve(f));
    } else for (Function &f : M)
      if (f.hasAddressTaken() && (!type || f.getFunctionType() == type) &&
          !markers().count(f.getName().str()))
        r.insert(resolve(&f));
  }
  return r;
}
struct Leaf {
  std::vector<unsigned> path;
  uint64_t offset;
  Type *type;
};
static std::vector<Leaf> leaves(Type *t, const DataLayout &dl) {
  std::vector<Leaf> result;
  std::function<void(Type *, std::vector<unsigned>, uint64_t)> walk =
      [&](Type *ty, std::vector<unsigned> p, uint64_t offset) {
        if (auto *st = dyn_cast<StructType>(ty)) {
          auto *layout = dl.getStructLayout(st);
          for (unsigned i = 0; i < st->getNumElements(); ++i) {
            auto q = p;
            q.push_back(i);
            walk(st->getElementType(i), q,
                 offset + layout->getElementOffset(i));
          }
        } else if (auto *at = dyn_cast<ArrayType>(ty)) {
          for (unsigned i = 0; i < at->getNumElements(); ++i) {
            auto q = p;
            q.push_back(i);
void Analysis::loadPair(const Atom &a, const Slot &s, Type *t, uint64_t size, AV &r, bool &partial) {
  if (s.filled && a.stride == 0 && s.stride == 0 &&
      a.offset >= s.offset &&
      (__int128)a.offset + size <= (__int128)s.offset + s.bytes &&
      (t->isIntegerTy() || t->isPointerTy() || t->isFloatingPointTy())) {
    AV decoded;
    decoded.top = s.value.top;
    for (auto &x : s.value.atoms)
      if (x.kind == Atom::Int) {
        APInt bits(M.getDataLayout().getTypeSizeInBits(t).getKnownMinValue(), 0);
        for (unsigned b = 0; b < size; ++b)
          bits |= x.integer.zextOrTrunc(bits.getBitWidth()).shl(b * 8);
        decoded.merge(integer(bits));
      }
    r.absorb(decoded);
    return;
  }
  bool exact = a.stride == 0 && s.stride == 0 && a.offset == s.offset &&
               size == s.bytes;
  bool sameClass = size == s.bytes &&
                   ((a.offset - s.offset) % (int64_t)size == 0) &&
                   (a.stride == 0 || a.stride % (int64_t)size == 0) &&
                   (s.stride == 0 || s.stride % (int64_t)size == 0);
  if (exact || sameClass)
    r.absorb(s.value);
  else {
    partial = true;
    if (s.value.atoms.hasRoot()) {
      AV roots;roots.atoms=s.value.atoms.rootsOnly();
      r.absorb(roots);
    }
  }
}
// The memo's group, against the slots its object changed since the memo's version. False when
// only a full pass gives PT6's result: a change that is not growth, or a group whose result a
// constant image's exact bytes decide (that decision can turn as slots grow).
bool Analysis::loadDelta(LoadMemo &memo, const Value *object, Type *t, uint64_t size) {
  if (!memo.keep.uid()) return false;
  bool perAtom = false;
  if (auto *g = dyn_cast<GlobalVariable>(object); g && g->isConstant() && t->isIntegerTy()) {
    if (memo.perAtom.size() != memo.atoms.size() || memo.atoms.empty()) {++rebuildsConstant;return false;}

### 5.3 Value sets

The **value set** VS(v) of an integer or pointer operand is the set of its `INT` and `FN` atoms
(pointer atoms denote addresses that are not compile-time constants and make VS(v) = ⊤ for
dispatch purposes), or ⊤. A value set is a precision tool only:

- an operand with a finite VS is dispatched over it (§3.4, §14.2);
- an operand with VS = ⊤ is dispatched over its type's whole valid domain where that domain is
  finite: operation codes and classes (§3.4), binding kinds (§8.4), the function addresses of a
  type (§7.2, §14.2);
- otherwise it is a blocker case of §25.1: widths (BLK-1), budgets computed on the device
  (BLK-2), parameters of in-place pairs (§8.7).

### 5.4 Uses

| Result | Read by |
|---|---|
| `FN` atoms of region operands | region candidate sets (§7.2) |
| `FN` atoms of indirect callees | indirect-call candidate sets (§14.2) |
| `ROOT` atoms | handle provenance (§6.2), handle containment (§6.4), static register files (§6.5) |
| `INT` atoms of descriptor and budget fields | descriptor shapes (§8.4), budget pre-minting (§9.5) |
| slots stored under control | the may-Co analysis and arena placement (§10, TDL-2, TDL-4) |
| objects loaded by L code | the oracle read set R and entry-state transfer (§8.5) |
| `INT` atoms of `op`, `cls`, `n` | dispatch domains (§3.4) |

### 5.3 Value sets

The **value set** VS(v) of an integer or pointer operand is the set of its `INT` and `FN` atoms
(pointer atoms denote addresses that are not compile-time constants and make VS(v) = ⊤ for
dispatch purposes), or ⊤. A value set is a precision tool only:

- an operand with a finite VS is dispatched over it (§3.4, §14.2);
- an operand with VS = ⊤ is dispatched over its type's whole valid domain where that domain is
  finite: operation codes and classes (§3.4), binding kinds (§8.4), the function addresses of a
  type (§7.2, §14.2);
- otherwise it has a run-time lowering: widths (§3.4, ISA K1), budgets computed on the device
  (§9.5, ISA K3), parameters of in-place pairs (§8.7, ISA K4 or the CC expansion).

### 5.4 Uses

| Result | Read by |
|---|---|
| `FN` atoms of region operands | region candidate sets (§7.2) |
| `FN` atoms of indirect callees | indirect-call candidate sets (§14.2) |
| `ROOT` atoms | handle provenance (§6.2), handle containment (§6.4), static register files (§6.5) |
| `INT` atoms of descriptor and budget fields | descriptor shapes (§8.4), budget pre-minting (§9.5) |
| slots stored under control | the may-Co analysis and arena placement (§10, TDL-2, TDL-4) |
| objects loaded by L code | the oracle read set R and entry-state transfer (§8.5) |
| `INT` atoms of `op`, `cls`, `n` | dispatch domains (§3.4) |

# Mixed fat object — shared contract scope

Proposal for every revision in this directory; not a published ISA amendment.
Clause references are to [ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md),
[the North Star](../../../../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md), and, where
qualified, the maintained dialect 0.2/0.3 texts. This is the shared statement
required by bennett-4ht and the council rubric's standing mixed-fat-object check.

## Citable scoping statement

> A mixed fat object retains an ordinary native host binary and embeds ISA
> objects for marked device modules and their linked device closures (North
> Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). ISA §§9.1–9.3 identify each device object and selected
> root; §12.3 certificates, owner records and reports cover that closure,
> with a separate binding/context set per device root and [] relative to
> that root (§12.1). Native entry, argv/envp and host-only libc need no ISA
> region, frame or certificate record; native comparison binds the selected
> materialised closure, not the whole host. Today §10 launches a device CC
> entry with copied arguments/entropy; §5.4 budgets bind device invocations,
> §3.3/§10 handles belong to a loaded program/run, and §7.3/§10 tapes cover
> device R effects. These require explicit container-to-object/root/certificate
> association, host-entry-to-device-entry bindings, budget/handle lifetime
> negotiation and tape/run association in a versioned mixed-launch contract.
> ISA §7.1 defines device memory but §10 defines no general host-memory-by-reference
> launch ABI: that contract must add address/layout mapping, range/liveness,
> aliasing, ownership/synchronisation, writeback and reversal rules, preserving
> dialect §§6.6, 8.5, 19.4–19.5. Until published, these are explicit
> bennett-4ht boundary obligations, not grounds to require whole-binary
> lowering or omit mixed execution.

## Present assumptions and exact publication changes

| Contract object | Assumption today | Exact change needed for mixed use |
|---|---|---|
| Object and linking (§§9.1–9.3) | One ISA object's closed sections and linked symbols; no native-container schema. | Publish a versioned inventory associating embedded/linked ISA object bytes and object_hash with selected device roots and certificates. A native-container hash, if used, is a separate identity. |
| Certificates/inventory (§§12.1–12.3) | Closure-keyed device-region records; current text does not explicitly delimit native inventory. | Insert the shared scoping rule in §12.3, cross-reference from §9.2, and require complete device-owner/link-proof coverage per root. Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.

// PT6 for the atoms of one object. Pure in those atoms, the type and the object's slots.
void Analysis::loadAtoms(const AtomSet &group, Type *t, uint64_t size, AV &r) {
  AV::startAbsorbing();
  for (auto &a : group) {
      bool partial = false;
      for (size_t n:overlappingSlots(a,size))
        loadPair(a, slots[n], t, size, r, partial);
      // Immutable initialiser bytes reconstruct whole integer loads exactly.
      if (auto *g = dyn_cast<GlobalVariable>(a.object);
          g && g->isConstant() && t->isIntegerTy() && a.stride == 0) {
        // Only the object's own slots can hold these bytes, and only those at or
        // below each byte: a byte-indexed initialiser costs its access, not its size.
        APInt bits(t->getIntegerBitWidth(), 0);
        bool known = true;
        for (unsigned b = 0; b < size; ++b) {
          bool found = false;
          Atom byte=a;byte.offset=a.offset+b;
          auto covering=overlappingSlots(byte,1);std::sort(covering.begin(),covering.end());
          for (size_t index : covering) {
            auto &s=slots[index];
            if (s.object == a.object && s.stride == 0 &&
                s.offset <= a.offset + b &&
                a.offset + b < s.offset + (int64_t)s.bytes &&
                s.value.atoms.size() == 1) {
              auto &x = *s.value.atoms.begin();
              if (x.kind != Atom::Int)
                continue;
              unsigned bi = a.offset + b - s.offset;
              unsigned shift = M.getDataLayout().isLittleEndian()
                                   ? bi * 8
                                   : (s.bytes - 1 - bi) * 8;
              uint64_t value = x.integer.lshr(shift).getLimitedValue() & 255;
              unsigned dest = M.getDataLayout().isLittleEndian()
                                  ? b * 8
                                  : (size - 1 - b) * 8;
              bits |= APInt(bits.getBitWidth(), value).shl(dest);
              found = true;
              break;
            }
          }
          known &= found;
        }
        if (known) {
          r.merge(integer(bits));
          partial = false;
        }
      }
      if (partial)
        r.top = true;
  }
  r.settle();
}
AV Analysis::load(const AV &p, Type *t) {
  AV r;
  uint64_t size = M.getDataLayout().getTypeStoreSize(t).getKnownMinValue();
  r.top = p.top;
  auto &groups=p.atoms.byObject();
  // Object versions only grow, so an unchanged sum means no object here changed: the whole
  // load is its last result. Most reruns of a load after a wakeup are this case.
  uint64_t versions=0;
  for(auto &group:groups)if(group.first)versions+=objectVersion[group.first];
  bool registered=false;
  if(currentStep>=0) {
    auto &seen=loadRegistered[currentStep];
    registered=seen==p.atoms.uid() && seen;
    seen=p.atoms.uid();
  }
  auto &whole=wholeLoads[{p.atoms.uid(),t}];
  if(whole.keep.uid() && whole.versions==versions) {
    if(!registered)for(auto &group:groups)if(group.first)readObject(group.first);
    AV result=whole.result;
    if(p.top && !result.top) {result.top=true;result.settle();}
    return result;
  }
  std::vector<LoadMemo *> memos;
  for(auto &group:groups) {
    const Value *object=group.first;
    if(!object) {r.top=true;continue;}
    if(!registered)readObject(object);
    uint64_t version=objectVersion[object];
    auto &memo=loadMemo[{group.second.uid(),t}];
    if(!memo.keep.uid() || memo.version!=version) {
  more = true;
  while (more) {
    more = false;
    for (auto &e : full)
      for (auto *g : e.second)
        if (reaches.count(g))
          more |= reaches.insert(e.first).second;
  }
  for (Function &f : M)
    if (reaches.count(&f) &&
        (f.onlyReadsMemory() || f.hasFnAttribute(Attribute::WillReturn) ||
         f.hasFnAttribute(Attribute::Speculatable)))
      fail("BD-E-EFFECT-LIE", "§3.8", &f);
}
void Analysis::checkHandles() {
  unsigned pw = M.getDataLayout().getPointerSizeInBits();
  for (Function &f : M)
    for (Instruction &i : instructions(f)) {
      for (unsigned k = 0; k < i.getNumOperands(); ++k) {
        Value *v = i.getOperand(k);
        if (!get(v).roots())
          continue;
        bool good = false;
        if (isa<PHINode>(i) || isa<FreezeInst>(i) || isa<ReturnInst>(i) ||
            isa<InsertValueInst>(i) || isa<ExtractValueInst>(i) ||
            isa<ShuffleVectorInst>(i))
          good = true;
        if (isa<InsertElementInst>(i))
          good = k != 2;
        if (isa<ExtractElementInst>(i))
          good = k == 0;
        if (isa<SelectInst>(i))
          good = k != 0;
        if (auto *c = dyn_cast<CastInst>(&i))
          good = (c->getOpcode() == Instruction::PtrToInt &&
                  c->getType()->isIntegerTy(pw)) ||
                 (c->getOpcode() == Instruction::IntToPtr &&
                  c->getOperand(0)->getType()->isIntegerTy(pw)) ||
                 (c->getOpcode() == Instruction::BitCast &&
                  M.getDataLayout().getTypeSizeInBits(c->getType()) ==
                      M.getDataLayout().getTypeSizeInBits(
                          c->getOperand(0)->getType()));
        if (auto *cmp = dyn_cast<ICmpInst>(&i))
          good = cmp->isEquality() &&
                 (get(cmp->getOperand(1 - k)).roots() ||
                  isa<ConstantPointerNull>(cmp->getOperand(1 - k)) ||
                  PatternMatch::match(cmp->getOperand(1 - k),
                                      PatternMatch::m_Zero()));
        if (auto *s = dyn_cast<StoreInst>(&i))
          good = k == 0;
        if (auto *c = dyn_cast<CallBase>(&i)) {
          Function *g = resolve(c->getCalledOperand());
          good = g && !g->isDeclaration() && k < c->arg_size();
          if (!g && k < c->arg_size()) {
            auto rr = candidates(c->getCalledOperand(), c->getFunctionType());
            good = !rr.empty();
            for (auto *target : rr)
              good &= !target->isDeclaration();
            if (!good)
              fail("BD-E-HANDLE-ESCAPE", "§6.4 opaque indirect argument", c);
          }
          if (g && markers().count(g->getName().str()))
            for (unsigned p : handleOperands(g->getName()))
              good |= k == p;
          if (!good && g && g->isDeclaration() &&
              !markers().count(g->getName().str()))
            fail("BD-E-HANDLE-ESCAPE", "§6.4 external argument", c);
        }
        if (!good)
          fail("BD-E-HANDLE-USE", "§6.2", &i);
      }
      if (auto *c = dyn_cast<CallBase>(&i))
        if (auto *g = c->getCalledFunction())
          for (unsigned p : handleOperands(g->getName()))
            if (!auditLegality() && !get(c->getArgOperand(p)).roots())
              fail("BD-E-HANDLE-UNTRACKED", "§6.2", c);
      if (auto *l = dyn_cast<LoadInst>(&i); l && get(l).roots()) {
        for (auto &leaf : leaves(l->getType(), M.getDataLayout()))
          if (field(l, leaf.path).roots()) {
            Type *lt = leaf.type;
            if (!lt->isPointerTy() && !lt->isIntegerTy(pw))
              fail("BD-E-HANDLE-USE", "§6.2 partial handle read", l);
            auto p = get(l->getPointerOperand());
            for (auto x : p.atoms) {
              if (x.kind == Atom::Ptr)
                x.offset += leaf.offset;
              for (auto &s : slots)
                if (s.value.roots() && overlaps(x, s,
                                                M.getDataLayout()
                                                    .getTypeStoreSize(lt)
                                                    .getKnownMinValue()))
                  if (s.bytes != pw / 8 ||
                      (x.stride == 0 && s.stride == 0 && x.offset != s.offset))
                    fail("BD-E-HANDLE-USE", "§6.2 overlapping read", l);
            }
          }
      }
      if (auto *r = dyn_cast<ReturnInst>(&i);
          r && r->getReturnValue() && get(r->getReturnValue()).roots() &&
          !f.hasLocalLinkage() && !tiers[&f].count("CC"))
        fail("BD-E-HANDLE-ESCAPE", "§6.4 exported return", r);
    }
  for (auto &s : slots)
  };
  if(AnalysisTrace)errs()<<"bennett: analysis: "<<order.size()<<" instructions, "<<slots.size()<<" seed slots, prepass "<<std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(std::chrono::steady_clock::now()-scope.budget.start).count()<<" ms\n";
  unsigned rounds = 0;

    if(seedsDue)solveSeeds();

        if(changed && rounds>=TraceChangesFrom && tracedChanges++<40) {

          if(!order[id]->getType()->isVoidTy()) {

              else {errs()<<" ["<<x.kind<<" off "<<x.offset<<" st "<<x.stride<<"]";}

json::Object Analysis::report() {
  json::Array funcs, regions, vals;
  for (Function &f : M)
    if (!f.isDeclaration()) {
      json::Array ts, es;
      for (auto &t : tiers[&f])
        ts.push_back(t);
      for (auto *g : edges[&f])
        es.push_back(g->getName().str());
      funcs.push_back(json::Object{{"name", f.getName().str()},
                                   {"tiers", std::move(ts)},
                                   {"callees", std::move(es)}});
      for (Instruction &i : instructions(f)) {
        AV a = get(&i);
        if (!i.getType()->isVoidTy()) {
          json::Array atoms;
          for (auto &x : a.atoms) {
            if (x.kind == Atom::Int) {
              SmallString<40> s;
              x.integer.toString(s, 10, false);
              atoms.push_back("INT(" + s.str().str() + ")");
            } else
              atoms.push_back(std::string(x.kind == Atom::Root ? "ROOT("
                                          : x.kind == Atom::Fn ? "FN("
                                                               : "PTR(") +
                              (x.object ? label(x.object) : "EXT") + "," +
                              std::to_string(x.offset) + "," +
                              std::to_string(x.stride) + ")");
          }
          vals.push_back(json::Object{{"function", f.getName().str()},
                                      {"value", label(&i)},
                                      {"top", a.top},
                                      {"atoms", std::move(atoms)}});
        }
        if (auto *c = dyn_cast<CallBase>(&i))
          if (auto *g = c->getCalledFunction())
            for (unsigned p : regionOperands(g->getName())) {
              json::Array rr;
              for (auto *r : candidates(c->getArgOperand(p)))
                rr.push_back(json::Object{
                    {"name", r->getName().str()},
                    {"key", r->isDeclaration() ? "external" : regionKey(r)}});
              regions.push_back(json::Object{{"function", f.getName().str()},
                                             {"marker", g->getName().str()},
                                             {"operand", (int64_t)p},
                                             {"candidates", std::move(rr)}});
            }
      }
    }
  json::Object report{{"dialect", "0.2.10"},
                      {"isa", "0.8"},
                      {"mode", "analysis-only"},

```llvm example=pt-variable-index verdict=accept
%struct.tab = type { [4 x ptr], i64 }

define void @t(i64 %i) {
  %s = alloca %struct.tab
  %h0 = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 1)
  store ptr %h0, ptr %s
  %pn = getelementptr %struct.tab, ptr %s, i64 0, i32 1
  store i64 3, ptr %pn
  %k = and i64 %i, 3
  %pk = getelementptr %struct.tab, ptr %s, i64 0, i32 0, i64 %k
  %hk = load ptr, ptr %pk
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %hk, i64 0)
  %n = load i64, ptr %pn
  %n2 = add i64 %n, 1
  store i64 %n2, ptr %pn
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h0)
  ret void
}
```

*(non-normative)* In `pt-variable-index`, `%pk` has the offset class (0 mod 8)[0, 32) in `%s`
(the `[4 x ptr]` array), so the load `%hk` reads the slots 0, 8, 16, 24: AV(`%hk`) =
{ROOT(`%h0`)}. The count field is at the exact offset 32, outside the class, so AV(`%n`) holds
INT(3) and, through the store of `%n2`, INT(4), INT(5), … until it exceeds K and becomes ⊤; it
holds no `ROOT` atom, and the arithmetic on it is legal (audit B-27: 0.1 gave the load offset `*`
and rejected this program).

**Finite-width and materialised-domain witnesses (round 6).** Under I=32,
2^32 truncates to zero, so the table load yields FN(body) and dispatch succeeds.
For the ranged inputs, N3/N4 erase the annotations: `n & 3` gives {0,1,2,3}
and `| 1` gives {1,3}. At n=2 width 3 admits both X gates on wire 2 and a clean
free. No new input precondition or fault is introduced.

# Binding interpretations of accepted dialect 0.2.10

Published with draft 0.2.12, 2026-11-01. Accepted text at `023f5a771a814270634db3be9cfde88ded69f975`, spec SHA-256 `d4f3c1a88391cc147f8c8fa4f80f82852e3ebe6f880610fc16cd5151f33d7206`, is unchanged; consumer pins remain valid. These interpretations resolve ambiguity in that text; new 0.2.12 ABIs/accuracy requirements are identified separately and are not retroactive ABI changes.

## mkb — §§5.2, 7.2, 12.2 N0

**N0 and resolution order.** Before testing the forwarding-thunk predicate or freezing candidates, the plugin MUST apply the N0 passes of §12.2 to the virtual device bodies used by S2; §7.2 resolution then uses those bodies, and §7.3 hashes their materialised closures.

The plugin MUST retain A(S) for every original source function or alias whose address reaches the region operand, dispatching that address to its post-N0 resolved candidate even when N0 removes the forwarding call; a declaration remaining after N0 is still F(8), but an intrinsic call removed by N0 MUST NOT turn its containing defined body into an external candidate.

## jjr — §§16.9, 17.1

The thin malloc body in §16.9 is a specialised witness whose calls all request 16 bytes. ISA allocation itself is exactly byte-bumping, not aligned; C malloc still owes its target ABI alignment. The general wrapper rounds its requested extent and inserts explicit charged padding allocations when an intervening runtime primitive leaves the next address unaligned, identically in both builds. This resolves the example-versus-C-ABI ambiguity without altering ISA §7.1.

## lbq — §§11.2–11.3, 14.7, 19.2

**Console syscall framing.** For virtual stdout/stderr, runtimes MUST implement `writev` by validating the vector count and total length before the first effect, then emitting one `console.write` per nonempty vector in source order (zero-length writes emit no record), returning the total byte count; the descriptor ABI limits are 0..1024 vectors and total at most target SSIZE_MAX, with invalid counts/overflow returning EINVAL and a null nonempty vector table returning EFAULT.

The console shim MUST return ENOTTY for `ioctl(TIOCGWINSZ)`, ESPIPE for seeking, and success for closing a virtual stdout/stderr descriptor, with no extra device effect or terminal metadata; this close releases no host resource and does not disable the virtual byte sink.

**Model history.** R console writes MUST use only the ISA effect tape, while L model state is ordinary reversible memory initialised from descriptor bindings, private and discarding only under the oracle rules of §11.3; action console capacity exhaustion MUST fault before mutation under TDL-12, and a standalone native-model test undo log MUST NOT substitute for either tier's history. No second production model-history ABI is required.

**Process context.** Before entering `<sym>`, the driver/runtimes preparation MUST establish a static single-thread musl process context with the supplied argc/argv/envp bytes, a null-terminated argv and envp, `environ`, program name, initial fenv and the libc/stdio state needed by the linked program; comparison-only Linux `_start` is permitted, but Linux ELF crt/TLS/auxv entry is not required in device tiers.

For a C `main` entry, the runtimes entry wrapper MUST treat normal return as `exit(status)`, running registered exit handlers and flushing stdio exactly once under §14.7 before the launch stub catches the exit pseudo-exception; explicit `exit` follows that same path, and `_Exit` bypasses handlers/flush as §14.7 specifies.

## 0na — §§3.1, 17.7, 19.5

The “nobody: primitives” ownership row applies to production bitcode before ISA lowering, not native comparison support implementing those primitives after materialisation. Such support must preserve device layout and history. Draft 0.2.12 §19.5 publishes the new v1 layout transport ABI; that spelling is not imposed retroactively on 0.2.10. The existing memory/fault outcomes and North Star definedness classifiers are not waived by UBSan-only support.

## dfd — §§13.1, 13.5

The explicit exact-conversion and correct-rounding guarantees take priority over APFloat implementation fallbacks. PPCDoubleDouble denotes high+low, not the legacy consecutive-106-bit approximation. A low component may not be discarded when the exact sum is representable in the destination. Sparse-tail loss and intermediate unsigned-low saturation are not permitted by the named-format reference. Draft 0.2.12 §13.1 makes canonical-pair rounding and flags explicit; no claim that the current arith publication already meets these guarantees is made.

## 1av — §§13.4–13.5

“No correct-rounding claim” does not authorise an arbitrary deterministic non-numerical substitute for the named operation or waive the specified IEEE flags. The accepted text does not quantify its numerical error. The one-local-ulp carrier bound and precise constrained accuracy/flag policy in draft 0.2.12 are NEW requirements, not retroactive assertions that 0.2.10 contained a numeric bound or that measured sweeps prove a full-domain bound.

## z7u — §13.1 arbitrary-format row

“Format named by metadata” means the LLVM major’s named interpretation MDString and its format/carrier semantics, not an unbounded user-created APFloat precision/exponent schema. LLVM 23 Intrinsics.td and LangRef define to(value, format, rounding, saturation) and from(bits, format). Draft 0.2.12 publishes NEW concrete carrier operand spellings and a static request/manifest protocol; those new ABI details are not claimed to have existed in 0.2.10.

## 21a — §§13.1, 13.5

The generic “soft-float call of §13.1 with suffix _rm” does not merge the distinct LLVM quiet fcmp and signaling fcmps operations. Quiet-NaN invalid behavior must remain distinguishable. Draft 0.2.12 publishes the NEW cmps_B_rm spelling and exact operand/exception lowering in §13.5; the absence of an earlier spelling was a contract gap, not authority to silently substitute quiet comparison.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:12020:| `n-libm-{x86,arm,riscv}-*`, `libm-invalid-casts` | N33 own-format carriers, §13.4 | parse, normalise, state | LLVM 18/23 per-triple verifier; host carrier numerical execution |
| `IN` | 1 | `iW` or `ptr addrspace(a)` (W = P(a)) | q with n(q) ≤ W | the parameter's value is the basis value of q, zero-extended (`mode` = ZEXT 0) or sign-extended (`mode` = SEXT 1) to W; a `ptr` parameter may be `IN` (a superposed pointer) | an `ins` bundle `x<k>:wire[n(q)]` (a pseudo-location, ISA §3.7), extended by `lift.zext`/`lift.sext` in the wrapper |
| `ARG` | 2 | `iW` (W ≤ 128) or `ptr` | none (`q` ignored) | a classical value: bits 0–63 from `value`, bits 64–127 from `size`; a `ptr` ARG is an address in the caller's classical memory, which the oracle reads through its entry state (§8.5) | an `args` entry `a<k>:i<W>` (W = P(a) for `ptr addrspace(a)`; ISA §4.7, §5.1) |
| `MEM_IN` | 3 | `ptr` | q with n(q) = 8·2^A, `size` = 2^A | the parameter is the base address of an interface region of 2^A bytes whose cell i is byte i of q (bits [8i, 8i + 8)); its contents may be superposed; role `in` (restored on every basis configuration, ISA §5.1). This is the quantum-addressed memory of NS §8 rung 4 | a `mem` region `m<k>:region[A,8] in`; the argument is `lift.addr @m<k>` |
| `OUT` | 4 | `ptr` | q with n(q) = 8·`size` | the parameter points to a fresh zero-filled buffer of `size` bytes in the oracle's memory; at exit its final bytes are XORed into q, byte k into wires [8k, 8k + 8) (a declared output, NS §4.2) | `lift.alloc size` in the wrapper; the bytes are part of the payload (§8.6) |
| `INOUT` | 5 | `iW` | q with n(q) = W | `inplace` only (§8.7): the in-place register x | `qinplace … x` |

The **result**: a non-null `tgt` register receives the return value: payload bits = the return
value zero-extended to n(tgt) if n(tgt) ≥ R's width; if n(tgt) is narrower, the dropped bits are
checked and an execution on which they are nonzero faults with code 4 (`FAULT_UB_TRAP`); the
retained bits are the low n(tgt). A null `tgt` discards the return value. For `oracle`, the
**fault register** `fault` MUST be a register of exactly 4 wires (ISA §5.4: F = 4) and MUST NOT
be null (`T-IFACE`).
Null `tgt` remains valid for a `void` function or a discarded non-void return.
If there is no `OUT` or non-null `CONSOLE` output either, the shape has zero payload
outputs; it still executes once and delivers its fault through §8.6's synthetic variant.
Every register named by the descriptor MUST be live and pairwise distinct (`T-DEAD`, `T-ALIAS`).
A width mismatch in the table above is `T-SIZE`.

### 8.3 Device bindings

`dev[j]` binds a device model for the lifted (coherent) tier (§11.3):

| Model | Code | Register | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ENTROPY` | 1 | q with n(q) = 8·2^A | an `in` region `e0:region[A,8]` holding the entropy tape, possibly superposed (NS §8 rung 8: "the entropy device is replaced by a superposed register") |
| `BLOCK` | 2 | q with n(q) = 8·512·2^A′ | an `in` region `k0` holding the disk image of 2^A′ sectors; writes go to a lifted copy-on-write overlay in the oracle's heap |
| `CLOCK` | 3 | none | the clock model's initial value is `value` |
| `CONSOLE` | 4 | q with n(q) = 8·L, or null | the first L bytes the oracle writes to the console are XORed into q as an output (payload, §8.6); with a null q console bytes are discarded with the oracle's other writes |

At most one binding per model. A model without a binding is **unbound**: an effect on it in L
code faults with code 8 on the executions that reach it (§11.3; NS §4.2).

### 8.4 Shapes and dispatch

or address". ⊤ never stands for a `ROOT` atom (handle provenance is always explicit); for a
function-pointer operand it stands for "any function of the operand's type whose address is
used" (§7.2, §14.2). Every slot (X, o) of memory has a content AV(X, o) of the same form. When A
holds more than **K = 4096** `INT` atoms they are dropped and t is set (a normative constant: it
decides dispatch and blocker verdicts, so §24 does not defer it). "AV(v) = ⊤" below means t is
set; unions and inclusions are taken component-wise.

Abstract-object and return propagation MUST cover attributed allocator calls and defined allocator wrappers through their underlying allocation sites without requiring the wrapper's name in a whitelist. [deps: DLT-5.1-P4, DLT-5.1-P6, DLT-5.2-P10, ISA-7.1, ISA-7.2]

+For these guarantees, a finite canonical double-double is a nonoverlapping pair (h,l) of binary64 values with h = roundTiesToEven(h+l) and l the exact residual (positive zero when the residual is zero), zero represented as (signed zero,+0); the result MUST be the canonical pair whose exact sum is nearest the exact mathematical result (or its directed neighbour for §13.5), with nearest ties resolved as follows. Let L and U be the adjacent canonical finite values bracketing the exact result, with L < U. If either low component is nonzero, compare the parities of BOTH low-component integer significands, treating a zero significand as even. Otherwise compare the high-component integer significands. The integer significand is the binary64 significand including the implicit leading bit for a normal value and without it for a subnormal; its sign does not affect parity. Choose the candidate of even parity when exactly one is even; if the parities coincide, choose the candidate whose exact sum has smaller absolute value. Existing exact-zero sign and overflow rules apply separately. This tie rule applies wherever this section requires nearest-even canonical-pair rounding, including incoming IEEE conversions, FMA and nearest-even carrier transcendentals of §13.4. Directed and nearest-away modes retain their specified rules. NaNs and infinities are represented in the high component with low +0. Overflow is determined by rounding with an unbounded high exponent first and then applying binary64's high-component exponent limit with IEEE overflow outcomes for the active mode (the finite maximum or infinity as appropriate); underflow uses tininess after rounding of the exact pair sum, with subnormal threshold 2^-1022.
 
 Cross-format conversion MUST use the exact sum of both components, be exact whenever that sum is representable in the destination, and otherwise round once to the destination format under the requested mode; it MUST NOT drop a representable low component, saturate an in-range positive total because its low component is negative, or introduce intermediate-conversion flags. For example, high = 1 and low = 2^-60 convert to x86_fp80 as exactly 1 + 2^-60, not 1. The `fpext` exactness statement describes true numerical widening; a cross-format pair whose value sets are not nested still uses this conversion rule, regardless of equal storage width.
 
 Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part. Other PPC operations retain their named major-specific APFloat semantics subject to the explicit canonical-NaN rules above.
 
+Both following witnesses use nearest-even mode (R=0). For u=2^-1074 and h=1+2^-52, fma((h,u),(1/2,+0),(h/2,+0)) returns (h,+0). Conversion of binary128 bits 0x3c030000000000001200000000000000 to PPC returns (binary64 bits 0x0030000000000001,+0). Each constrained operation, with initial flags zero and exceptions observed, raises exactly inexact (16); observed exceptions OR 16 into existing sticky flags. Unconstrained operations return the same bits without modifying the floating-point environment. Negated exact inputs return the corresponding negative high with low +0, again raising exactly 16 when observed. The equal-parity fallback is total: L=-U with L<U cannot be an adjacent canonical pair, since the canonical zero pair lies strictly between any negative and positive canonical sums. Suite DF's tagged `ppc-zero-low-parity` vector checks these midpoint/selector policies and two-zero-low subnormal neighbours; full-domain arithmetic conformance remains a producer obligation.

 Cross-format conversion MUST use the exact sum of both components, be exact whenever that sum is representable in the destination, and otherwise round once to the destination format under the requested mode; it MUST NOT drop a representable low component, saturate an in-range positive total because its low component is negative, or introduce intermediate-conversion flags. The `fpext` exactness statement describes true numerical widening; a cross-format pair whose value sets are not nested still uses this conversion rule, regardless of equal storage width.
 
 Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part. Other PPC operations retain their named major-specific APFloat semantics subject to the explicit canonical-NaN rules above.
 
+Both following witnesses use nearest-even mode (R=0). For u=2^-1074 and h=1+2^-52, fma((h,u),(1/2,+0),(h/2,+0)) returns (h,+0). Conversion of binary128 bits 0x3c030000000000001200000000000000 to PPC returns (binary64 bits 0x0030000000000001,+0). Each constrained operation, with initial flags zero and exceptions observed, raises exactly inexact (16); observed exceptions OR 16 into existing sticky flags. Unconstrained operations return the same bits without modifying the floating-point environment. Negated exact inputs return the corresponding negative high with low +0, again raising exactly 16 when observed. The equal-parity fallback is total: L=-U with L<U cannot be an adjacent canonical pair, since the canonical zero pair lies strictly between any negative and positive canonical sums. Suite DF's tagged `ppc-zero-low-parity` vector checks these midpoint/selector policies and two-zero-low subnormal neighbours; full-domain arithmetic conformance remains a producer obligation.
+
 ### 13.2 Vector floating point
 
 After N21 every vector floating-point operation is scalar: §13.1 applies lane by lane, and
@@ -6988,6 +6990,30 @@ Suite DF checks the independent §§13.1/13.4/13.5 boundary vectors in `scripts/
 
 ### 13.6 Examples
 
+The following DF vector binds an independent integer selector fixture; exact rational midpoint and adjacency checks precede native execution. It is not production arithmetic conformance. The equal-parity branch cases are synthetic selector tests, not assertions of adjacent canonical candidates.

+```text state=ppc-zero-low-parity at=0
+{
+  "source_sha256": "c7bef159ddba2e3ff9229c0dac7f2bfb3cac23cf9aace01bfaad327f07809833",

 The unordered bit is predicate bit 3: ueq = 8|1 = 9, uno = 8, une = 8|2|4 = 14.
 
 ```llvm normalise-in=n-fcmp
@@ -10033,6 +10059,54 @@ The §16.9 thin wrapper is a specialised witness for its only allocation size, 1
 
 Suites DR/DD check the §§17.1/19.5 allocation/layout boundary vector in `scripts/0212_allocation.c` and `scripts/dialect_0212_vectors.py`; this is a conformance vector, not a new primitive.
 
+The allocation vector fixes the trace of its stated thin wrapper, not a universal trace for every implementation of malloc/calloc/realloc. Runtime bodies may retain requested size, liveness and alignment state in ordinary materialised memory, including dynamically allocated metadata and explicit zero-size address probes. Every such operation, its stores, padding and allocation history is present identically in device and native materialisation and charged to the actual heap/step/table budgets. The C payload request retains the rounded extent and returned-base rule above; padding immediately before that payload is minimal from the actual current primitive bump. No hidden header, private ISA-table query, native-only map or fixed metadata cap is implied or permitted by this allowance. Reusing live metadata storage as ordinary data does not reclaim primitive payload addresses or allocation-table/history entries.
+
+realloc preserves min(old requested size,new size) bytes of a live C allocation, using retained ordinary metadata or another published mechanism. Old requested size means the original C byte count, including the checked calloc product, excluding ABI rounding, padding and metadata: malloc(1) followed by realloc(p,48) preserves one byte, not the rounded 16-byte extent. Invalid/dead/interior pointers retain the materialised memory/fault contract. Overflow preflights required by the C allocation alignment and padding rules above precede the corresponding allocation mutation; a later primitive failure retains the successfully executed ordinary prefix unless explicitly reversed. Both comparison builds execute the same body, including its failure prefix. Tests of a production runtime bind that body's identity and exact resulting trace; they do not impose the thin fixture's record count on a different body. Prefix retention describes forward execution before cleanup or reversal and does not waive oracle workspace restoration or the version-specific R-fault provisions of §§17.5–17.6.
+
+In a mixed fat object, this bookkeeping allowance governs the allocator closure selected for a marked device entry and that closure's native comparison build. The surrounding native host binary, native entry, argv/envp and host-only libc remain outside that device object, subject to the existing placement/promotion rules of §§4.2–4.4 and 19.4. Allocation traces, budgets and runtime-body identities bind that selected closure. Shared caller allocation state, including ordinary allocator metadata bytes and primitive allocation history, is transferred and charged under §8.5; boundary addresses and layout follow §§19.4–19.5. Neither a whole-program device entry nor a new allocation query is required by this clarification. Section 8.5 specifies oracle entry-state snapshot/replay, not a general host-memory-by-reference launch ABI; container/root/certificate association, host/device entry bindings, budget/handle lifetime negotiation, tape/run association, address/layout mapping, range/liveness, aliasing, ownership/synchronisation, writeback and reversal remain explicit mixed-launch contract obligations (bennett-4ht).
+
+A zero-size primitive probe consumes one heap byte and one allocation-table entry, records requested size zero and supplies no accessible payload bytes. The separate tagged `allocator-bookkeeping` witness uses ordinary 32-byte metadata, empty lists, alignment 16, heap base 4096 and an initial raw alloc(3). Its requests are [3,0,12,16,32]; heap=64 returns 4112 with final bump 4160, five records and code 0. Heap=32 does not return from the wrapper: it faults with code 2 at bump 4128 after four successful records. These allocation-only totals assume sufficient other resources and no additional prologue allocations; replay and shadow-stack backing are charged separately when present. Primitive-only histories alloc(16);alloc(32) and alloc(32);alloc(16) have the same first pointer p and final bump p+48 but different old sizes; the cursor alone cannot supply realloc's old requested size. The identity-bound finite bookkeeping fixture checks actual metadata stores, failure prefixes, reversal and replay; production runtimes must supply the same checks against their own pinned bodies.

# Bennett LLVM Dialect — Specification, draft 0.2.13

**Date:** 2026-11-03. **Status:** published 0.2.13 under maintainer D3 (2026-11-03), incorporating council-accepted 2cg and 4yg.1 (2026-11-02; no blocking items). Supersedes 0.2.12; its binding 5u3 interpretations in `errata-0.2.12.md` continue to apply.
The preceding 0.2.12 superseded 0.2.11, accepted by the council's sol and codex seats in round 10
(2026-10-31; gate 2834/2834), retained at git `eaad067` with accepted-text SHA-256
`05101a18e04098cfca5fdfb52edc9d9027723455adf2569da2fa49ef7a47c7c1`.
Its pre-acceptance freeze receipt records spec SHA-256
`67157631837887fabb412b762d571157f71dbb79bb9ff13331ec692ce7ad82ba`.
Accepted 0.2.10 and its SHA are retained by `errata-0.2.10.md`; its text is unchanged.
The grading target remains Bennett ISA **0.8**. [deps: DLT-front-P2, DLT-1.3, DLT-23]

**Versioning.** Dialect 0.2.13: markers 0.2, handles 0.2.8, regions 0.2.8, oracle-abi 0.2.4, tdl 0.2.4, effects 0.2.4, norm 0.2.8, map 0.2.9, asm 0.2, platform 0.2.1, driver 0.2.1, corpus 0.2; targets ISA 0.8. [deps: DLT-front-P2, DLT-23]
The **full version string** (`0.2.13`) is the compatibility unit (§23): drafts are not compatible with each other. [deps: DLT-front-P2, DLT-23]
This publication retains existing marker signatures and the canonical lifting map. Its explicit semantic revisions are the PPC tie decision (§13.1) and body-dependent allocator bookkeeping (§17.1); other reference behaviour is retained. [deps: DLT-front-P2, DLT-3.2, DLT-16, DLT-23]
Declared dependency markers are retained for historical context; the council withdrew the requirement on 2026-10-31 (CLAUDE.md rule 12).
ISA 0.9 adoption remains in the separate 0.3 track described by `outline-0.3.md`, D15–D19; this draft does not adopt those operations.

over unmodified SQLite is the core). It rejects at compile time only what the north star itself

  // A chain of displaced addresses (a pointer recurrence through memory, or a copy between
  // offsets of one object) denotes PT2's modular orbit, which only ends after 2^60 or so
  // elements. Take the orbit, the residue class of the differences' lowest set bit with PT2's
  // unknown-address alternative, past K offsets of one provenance: the bound §5.1 sets on an
  // index set, so an index domain within K keeps its exact offsets. §5.1 states K for INT atoms
  // only; b-dialect-spec-5u3 asks it to state the offset bound too.
  // Only under -bennett-analysis-early-chain-close (not the reference): also past 4 offsets
  // outside the object, and past 32 of an object of no literal extent.
  constexpr size_t OffsetChain=4096,EarlyLeftObject=4,EarlyNoExtent=32;
  const size_t LeftObject=EarlyChainClose?EarlyLeftObject:SIZE_MAX,NoExtent=EarlyChainClose?EarlyNoExtent:SIZE_MAX;
  size_t pointers=firstStar-ids.begin();
  bool chained=false;
  if(closeChains && pointers>std::min<size_t>(OffsetChain,LeftObject)) {
    struct Tally {uint32_t total=0,outside=0;uint64_t extent=0;bool known=false;};
    llvm::DenseMap<const Value *,Tally> counts;
    for(auto it=ids.begin();it!=firstStar && !chained;++it) {
      const Atom &a=table.atoms[*it&IndexMask];
      if(a.kind!=Atom::Ptr)continue;
      auto &t=counts[a.object];
      if(!t.known) {t.known=true;t.extent=objectExtent(a.object);}
      ++t.total;
      if((uint64_t)a.offset>t.extent)++t.outside; // One past the end is an ordinary address.
      chained=t.total>OffsetChain || t.outside>LeftObject || (t.extent==UINT64_MAX && t.total>NoExtent) || collapsedObjects().count(a.object);
    }
  }
  if(chained) {
    struct Provenance {const Value *object;bool numerical;bool operator<(const Provenance &o) const {
      return object!=o.object?std::less<const Value *>{}(object,o.object):numerical<o.numerical;}};
    std::map<Provenance,std::vector<uint32_t>> parts;
    for(auto it=ids.begin();it!=firstStar;++it) {
      const Atom &a=table.atoms[*it&IndexMask];
      if(a.kind==Atom::Ptr)parts[{a.object,a.numerical}].push_back(*it);
    }
    std::vector<uint32_t> doomed,added;
    for(auto &part:parts) {
      uint64_t extent=objectExtent(part.first.object),outside=0;
      for(uint32_t id:part.second)outside+=(uint64_t)table.atoms[id&IndexMask].offset>extent;
      bool collapsed=collapsedObjects().count(part.first.object);
      if(!collapsed && part.second.size()<=OffsetChain && outside<=LeftObject && !(extent==UINT64_MAX && part.second.size()>NoExtent))continue;

      for(uint32_t id:part.second) {
        const Atom &a=table.atoms[id&IndexMask];
        if(a.stride>0)low|=(uint64_t)a.stride;
        low|=(uint64_t)a.offset-(uint64_t)first.offset;
      }
      Atom closed=first;
      unsigned zeros=low?llvm::countr_zero(low):63;
      if(zeros==0 || collapsed) {closed.offset=0;closed.stride=-1;}
      else {closed.stride=int64_t(1)<<zeros;closed.offset=(int64_t)((uint64_t)first.offset&((uint64_t)closed.stride-1));}
      doomed.insert(doomed.end(),part.second.begin(),part.second.end());
      added.push_back(table.intern(closed));
